`report` is `portfolio_overview`, `unrealised_gains`, or `performance`. `POST /report_snapshots/generate` returns `200` with the three stored snapshots' metadata, or `422` with the blocker detail when the day cannot be trusted: a held listing's price is missing (backfill it) or errored (re-fetch it), the date's close is not final yet, an FX-rate gap too old for the valuation fallback, or nothing was held on the date. The two regeneration endpoints return `200` with `{ "regenerated": ["YYYY-MM-DD", …], "blocked": [{ "date", "reason" }, …] }` — a blocked date is reported there rather than failing the request, and its stored snapshot is left as it was. `POST /report_snapshots/regenerate_all` additionally returns `422` if its resolved `from` is after its `to`.

## Jobs

Recurring maintenance jobs scheduled from the cron file (see [Scheduled maintenance](../README.md#scheduled-maintenance)). These endpoints inspect the registered jobs and trigger them on demand.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/jobs` | List registered jobs (sorted) with each one's run history |
| `POST` | `/jobs/:name?suffix=` | Run the named job now |

`GET /jobs` returns a JSON array (sorted by job name); each element is `{ "name", "trigger", "last_started_at", "last_finished_at", "last_success", "last_error", "runs" }`. `trigger` is how the job is meant to be started, recorded in the job registry itself: `"scheduled"` (it belongs on the cron schedule — a missing schedule line is a fault, and startup logs a `WARN` naming it) or `"manual_only"` (a deliberately schedule-less one-off repair, registered with `register_manual`, that runs only from `POST /jobs/:name` — startup says nothing about it, and the Jobs screen labels it **manual only** so a job that has never run does not read as an overdue one). The two manual-only jobs are `price-rebase` and `settlement-recompute`, described below. `runs` is the job's stored run history, most recent first; each entry is `{ "started_at", "finished_at", "success", "error" }` — the RFC 3339 start/finish timestamps, a boolean success flag, and the error text (`null` on success). Every run — scheduled or manual — appends a `job_runs` row, and the same write prunes that job's history to the newest 20 runs, so an intermittent failure that later succeeded stays diagnosable here without unbounded growth. The four `last_*` fields duplicate the newest entry for at-a-glance reading; they are `null` (and `runs` empty) for a job that has never run. The recorded error text is the failure's own message with the cause beneath it appended — e.g. `could not fetch the RBA FX rate feed: error sending request for url (https://www.rba.gov.au/statistics/tables/csv/f11-data.csv): client error (Connect): tcp connect error: Connection refused (os error 61)` — so a failed feed fetch says both what failed and why (the transport error's own message names only the URL).

`POST /jobs/:name` runs the job synchronously and returns `204 No Content` on success, `404 Not Found` if no job has that name, or `500 Internal Server Error` if the job fails. Either way the run is recorded (see `GET /jobs`). Runs of the same job are serialised: a trigger that overlaps an in-flight run of that job (scheduled or manual) waits for it to finish and then runs — the same job never executes concurrently. Registered jobs are `backup`, `rba-fx-import` (which also trues up provisional [report snapshots](#report-snapshots) when new rates land), `mic-import`, `currency-import`, `price-import` (see [Closing prices](#closing-prices); scheduled per market close — each run re-attempts, per held listing, every trading day in the last 14 calendar days whose stored row is missing or errored, never re-fetching a day stored ok, so the runs are idempotent and are each other's outage backfill), `report-snapshot` (see [Report snapshots](#report-snapshots); daily after the second price import, backfilling missing dates and regenerating stale/provisional ones over its 14-day window), `price-rebase` (see [Closing prices](#closing-prices); **not scheduled** — a one-off repair that re-derives every stored price from the figure it was observed as, for a database whose prices predate the contemporaneous-basis rule. Recording a split already does this for its own listing, so on any other database it changes nothing; it is idempotent either way), and `settlement-recompute` (**not scheduled** either — a repair to run after seeding a missing [exchange holiday](#exchange-holidays) year, which otherwise clears the [settlement holiday coverage](#settlement-holiday-coverage) report without correcting the settlement dates it flagged. It re-derives every **auto-calculated** settlement date from the calendar as it now stands; a `settlement_date` supplied on the write is the taxpayer's own assertion and is never rewritten, nor is one on a row that predates the provenance column (migration 0041). Idempotent: a run that finds nothing to correct writes nothing, and each date it does rewrite is logged with what it was. A stored date worth a second look for any other reason stays the coverage report's business — the job deliberately does not opine on the dates it may not rewrite, most of which are the derived paths' correct same-day settlements).

The optional `?suffix=` query param labels a one-off manual run — only the `backup` job reads it (the others accept and ignore it); it is appended to the backup's filename as `-<suffix>.db` (e.g. `?suffix=pre-0.5.1` before an upgrade). Allowed characters are ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, and `-`, up to 40 characters, and the suffix must not itself start with `-` or `.`; an invalid suffix returns `422 Unprocessable Entity` with a plain-text reason, rejected before the job runs — so a bad request never records a run in `GET /jobs`. `pkg/freebsd/update.sh` uses this to take a `pre-<version>` backup immediately before installing a new package, on top of the weekly scheduled backup.

The `backup` job writes a timestamped `VACUUM INTO` copy of the database, **verifies** it (the produced file must pass `PRAGMA integrity_check` and carry the same applied migrations as the live database), optionally runs the configured `--backup-command`/`backup_command` against it (see [Off-machine copies](../README.md#off-machine-copies)), and then **prunes** the backup destination to the retention policy — the newest 8 backups plus the first backup of each of the 12 most recent months; only files matching the backup naming pattern are ever deleted (see [Scheduled maintenance](../README.md#scheduled-maintenance)). A suffixed one-off backup is pruned by the same policy as any other — it is not exempt. A backup that fails verification is quarantined as `<name>.db.bad` and the run fails: `POST /jobs/backup` returns `500` and the verification failure is recorded as the run's `last_error` in `GET /jobs`. A configured post-backup command that exits non-zero likewise fails the run (its output is logged, not returned in the response) — but pruning still runs regardless, since the fresh backup is always within the retention window either way. A verified backup whose pruning step fails also fails the run (the backup file itself is good and keeps its name) — so a `500` from this endpoint always means the Jobs page has the reason.

## Trades

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/trades` | List all trades |
| `GET` | `/trades/:id` | Get one trade |
| `PUT` | `/trades/:id` | Create or update a trade |
| `DELETE` | `/trades/:id` | Delete a trade |

If `settlement_date` is omitted from the PUT body, it is auto-calculated by advancing `date` by `exchange.settlement_days` **business days** — both weekends and the exchange's seeded public holidays (see [Exchange holidays](#exchange-holidays)) are skipped. If the trade's settlement window falls outside the exchange's seeded holiday coverage, the calculation skips weekends only; the write still succeeds but logs a `WARN`, and the trade is flagged by the [Settlement holiday coverage](#settlement-holiday-coverage) report (the same applies to Sells entered via `PUT /sells/:id`). A trade on an exchange-less ([Crypto](#listings)) listing settles **same-day** instead: the auto-populated `settlement_date` is the trade date, with no holiday lookup and no coverage warning. A `settlement_date` **supplied** in the body is stored exactly as given: the only rule it has to satisfy is that it is not before `date` (`422` otherwise). It is deliberately not put to the exchange's calendar, so a settlement on a Saturday or a public holiday is accepted — an explicit value is a deliberate override the taxpayer is asserting, and refusing one would make an existing row uneditable. The [Settlement holiday coverage](#settlement-holiday-coverage) report flags it instead.

Which of the two wrote the stored date is recorded with it, as the read-only `settlement_date_source` field on every trade response: `computed` (the body omitted it and the server derived it from the calendar), `stated` (the body supplied it), or `unrecorded` (the row predates the column — migration 0041). It exists because only a **computed** date may be re-derived later by the [`settlement-recompute` job](#jobs); a stated one is an assertion, and an unrecorded one might be, so neither is ever rewritten. Re-supplying the date **already stored** — which is what a `GET` body PUT back verbatim does, and so what the web UI's edit form sends — leaves the recorded source as it was: handing us back our own value is not an assertion, and treating it as one would quietly opt every edited trade out of the repair. Supplying a *different* date is.

`PUT /trades/:id` rejects `trade_type: "Sell"` with `422` — Sells must be created via `PUT /sells/:id` (see below) so they are always persisted with a full set of parcel allocations.

`PUT /trades/:id` likewise rejects `trade_type: "DRP"` with `422` — DRP trades are created only via `POST /income/:id/reinvest` (see [Income](#income)), which links the reinvested shares back to their funding distribution (`income.reinvestment_trade_id`) and threads the residual carry-forward chain. A free-form DRP would be an orphan parcel (no income link, zero residuals) that could shadow that chain, and a re-`PUT` of a reinvest-created DRP would silently zero its residual columns; both are refused. A **`Buy` body targeting a reinvest-created DRP** is refused with `422` too — the distribution's link lives on the income row, so without the guard the write would silently re-type the trade and wipe its residual chain while the income row keeps pointing at it. Undo the reinvestment via its distribution (`DELETE /income/:id/reinvest`, see [DRP reinvestment](#drp-reinvestment)) and re-reinvest instead. (The endpoint still creates and edits plain `Buy` trades.)

`holding_account_id` is optional on the body and defaults to the seeded default account (1) — see [Holding accounts](#holding-accounts). The same default applies to the `holding_account_id` field on income, AMMA statement, DRP enrolment, and Sell writes, and on the rights-exercise, sell-rights, and buy-back participation operations.

Brokerage can be entered GST-inclusive, as broker statements quote it: set `brokerage_includes_gst: true` and put the inclusive amount in `brokerage` — the server splits it at write time (`gst_on_brokerage` = amount × 1/11 rounded to the cent, half away from zero; `brokerage` keeps the exact remainder, so the stored pair always sums back to the amount paid). Any `gst_on_brokerage` supplied alongside the flag is ignored — deriving it is the point. **Reads present the same shape writes expect (lossless round-trip):** with the flag set, `GET /trades` and `GET /trades/:id` return `brokerage` as the one GST-inclusive amount — the stored ex-GST brokerage and GST recombined, exactly what was entered — with `gst_on_brokerage` carrying the derived GST component (informational on reads; a flagged write ignores it). `brokerage` therefore means the GST-inclusive amount **on both reads and writes** whenever the flag is set, and `PUT`ting a `GET` response body back verbatim re-splits the same amount to the **identical stored pair** — a GET→edit→PUT client never shifts the figures. This holds for flagged Sells too (read via `GET /trades/:id`, written via `PUT /sells/:id`). With the flag off (the default), `brokerage` is ex-GST, `gst_on_brokerage` is entered manually, and reads return the stored values as-is.

An optional `statement_total` (decimal, in the brokerage currency) cross-checks the entry against the broker statement's net transaction total: when supplied it must equal `quantity × price + brokerage + GST` (the amount payable; compared numerically, so `1009.95` matches `1009.9500`) either exactly or rounded to the cent (half away from zero — contract notes print the consideration cent-rounded, so `1,302 × 37.585914 + 9.50 = 48,946.360028` accepts the note's `48,946.36`), and a mismatch beyond that sub-cent rounding is rejected with `422` whose body carries the figure the trade computes to. A single-currency total always exists to check against, because a trade's `brokerage_currency` must equal its `currency` (see below). The stored value is informational-only: no report or calculation uses it. Both fields apply to Sells too (see [Sells](#sells)), where the total is the **net proceeds**, `quantity × price − brokerage − GST`. Operation-created trades (DRP reinvestment, rights exercise, buy-back participation, scrip exchange, demerger, transfer, worthless-shares recognise) never carry the flag or a total.

An optional `spot_fx_rate` (decimal, foreign units per 1 AUD — the same convention as `fx_rate`) records the transaction-date spot rate as a deliberate override: when set it wins over the imported monthly RBA rate everywhere the trade's amounts convert to AUD (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion) for when the ATO says to use it). It is rejected with `422` when non-positive or supplied on an AUD-currency trade (an AUD amount never converts, so the override could only be a mistake silently ignored). Absent (`null`), conversion is unchanged: monthly rate first, `fx_rate` fallback. The field applies to Sells too (see [Sells](#sells)); the scrip-for-scrip exchange, demerger, and transfer operations carry a consumed parcel's override onto its replacement Buys so the carried AUD cost base is unchanged.

Core figures are sanity-checked at write time — a degenerate value would silently corrupt every downstream report: `quantity` must be positive, `average_price` / `brokerage` / `gst_on_brokerage` cannot be negative (a zero price is legitimate — e.g. nil proceeds), `fx_rate` must be a positive foreign-per-AUD rate (1 for AUD), `brokerage_currency` must equal `currency`, `settlement_date` cannot be before `date`, and `date` itself must fall between **20 September 1985** and **today** inclusive. Below the floor a pre-CGT holding is outside CGT and not modelled, so every report would wrongly compute a capital gain or loss on it (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)); above the ceiling a trade records a transaction that has not happened — nearly always a typo (a 2027-for-2026 slip on a July trade), and it would offer a financial year that has not begun on the [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report)'s year picker. `settlement_date` is deliberately **not** bounded above: a T+2 settlement of a trade dated today is legitimately in the future. A violation returns `422` naming the rule. The same checks apply to Sells (see [Sells](#sells)), and to the closing Sell each parcel-substituting operation writes through the same path (scrip-for-scrip exchange, demerger, holding-account transfer, buy-back participation, worthless-shares recognise): a [corporate action](#corporate-actions) may be **recorded** ahead of its effective date, but performing it is refused until that date arrives — the parcels it would create are dated the action.

**A trade's `date` must be a day its exchange actually traded.** `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` naming the day and the exchange (`the trade is dated on a day its exchange did not trade — 2026-05-16 is a Saturday on XASX`) for a weekend or a seeded [exchange holiday](#exchange-holidays), on the calendar in force **on that date** — so a listing that has since [changed exchange](#listings) is judged on the market it really traded on. The trade date is the CGT event date: it sets the 12-month discount clock, the financial year the gain falls in, and the day the T+n settlement count starts from, so a day the market was shut is a data-entry error by construction. This is the same calendar, and the same check, that already refuses a [closing price](#closing-prices) on a non-trading day. Exchange-less ([Crypto](#listings)) listings trade every day and are exempt; so is a year with no seeded holidays — only weekends are refused there. The same rule applies to Sells (see [Sells](#sells)).

The **derived** paths that write trade rows directly are deliberately **not** covered: an [ESS vest](#ess-statements), an [inherited parcel](#inheritances), a [DRP reinvestment](#drp-reinvestment), a [rights exercise](#corporate-actions) and every parcel-substituting operation are dated by facts that need not fall on a market day — a taxing point, a date of death, a corporate action's effective date. Those rows are surfaced non-blockingly instead, by `non_trading_day_trades` on the [health report](#health).

**A trade's `currency` must be its listing's** — `422` naming both otherwise. `average_price` *is* the price of that listed security, so the trade and the listing quote the same money: a US-quoted share bought "in AUD" divides an AUD price by a USD rate in every cost-base report, while its [closing prices](#closing-prices) — collected from the exchange in the listing's currency — value it against a parcel costed in another. This is the rule [ESS statements](#ess-statements) apply to a per-share market value, [inheritances](#inheritances) to a parcel's cost base, [return-of-capital actions](#corporate-actions) to a payment, and the [DRP reinvest](#drp-reinvestment) path to a distribution's cash; the same applies to Sells and to [AMMA statements](#amma-statements). A contract note in another currency is entered converted (as a foreign brokerage already is). The one exception is not enterable: a **rollover replacement parcel** (scrip-for-scrip, demerger, transfer) carries its consumed parcel's currency and FX rate onto the new listing so the carried AUD cost base is unchanged by the substitution, and those trades are written by the operation rather than through `PUT /trades`.

`brokerage_currency` records the currency the fee was billed in, and **must equal the trade's `currency`** — a differing pair is rejected with `422`. Every figure the brokerage feeds is a single-currency sum: a Buy/DRP's cost base is `price × quantity + brokerage + GST` in the trade's currency, converted to AUD as one figure at the acquisition month's rate ([FX conversion](#fx-conversion)); a Sell's proceeds are the consideration **net** of those costs before converting; and the [activity ledger](#activity-ledger)'s transaction total is the same sum. A fee billed in another currency (an Australian broker's AUD commission on a US trade) therefore has to be entered converted into the trade's own currency — converting it at the trade month's rate gives the identical AUD cost base, since the whole figure converts at that rate anyway. See [Known limitations](#known-limitations).

Buy/DRP trades carry the same write-time integrity as Sells (validated atomically in a transaction):

- `DELETE /trades/:id` returns `422` if the trade is still referenced — as the purchase parcel of a Sell's allocation, by an AMIT adjustment, as a distribution's reinvestment trade, or by a buy-back dividend income row (`income.buyback_trade_id`) — or if it belongs to a scrip-for-scrip exchange or demerger group (`scrip_action_id` / `demerger_action_id` set: the group is only ever deleted as a whole, via `DELETE /sells/:id` on its closing Sell), it is an [ESS vest Buy](#ess-statements) (`ess_statement_id` set: removed via `DELETE /ess_statements/:id`), or it is a [worthless-shares recognise closing Sell](#recognising-worthless-shares) (`worthless_action_id` set: removed via `DELETE /sells/:id`, which restores the holding) — instead of surfacing the FK error as `500`. Remove the dependants first (e.g. delete the Sell via `DELETE /sells/:id`).
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the edit would shrink the trade's `quantity` below what its dependants rely on: the total already allocated out to Sells (each allocation re-based to the parcel's as-acquired units across any [share splits/consolidations or bonus issues](#corporate-actions)), or any linked AMIT adjustment's covered quantity (AMIT adjustment quantities are expressed in the parcel's as-acquired units).
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if a Buy/DRP's `currency` differs from that of a [return of capital](#corporate-actions) recorded on its listing that reaches it (acquired before the payment's `record_date`, or on or before the payment date when none is recorded) — the payment reduces the parcel's cost base in the parcel's own currency, and amounts are never netted across currencies, so the reports could only fail loudly on the pair. The body names the payment's date and both currencies. This is the parcel side of the rejection `PUT /corporate_actions/:id` answers from the payment's side.
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the edit would change the trade's `listing_id` while Sell allocations or AMIT adjustments reference the parcel — accepting it would silently re-associate those dependants to the new listing, costing them cross-listing in the CGT reports. Remove the dependants first (e.g. delete the Sell via `DELETE /sells/:id`), or leave the listing unchanged.
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the edit would move the trade's `date` **after a Sell that allocates from it** — units can't be sold before they were acquired, and the pair would run the CGT discount clock backwards in every report. This is the parcel side of the Sell-path rejection (an allocation consuming a parcel dated after the sale date, see [Sells](#sells)); a move to the sale date itself, or to any earlier date, is fine. Remove the Sell first (`DELETE /sells/:id`), or fix the Sell's own date.
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the edit would change the trade's `holding_account_id` while Sell allocations or AMIT adjustments reference the parcel — a sale only disposes of units its own account holds and an AMMA statement only adjusts its own account's parcels, so accepting it would report the parcel as held in one account while its realised gain (or cost-base adjustment) stays costed against it in another. Move the parcel with a [Transfer](#transfers) instead, or remove the dependants first (e.g. `DELETE /sells/:id`).
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the existing trade is a rights exercise (`rights_action_id` set): its figures were validated against the rights issue's entitlement, which a free-form edit could exceed. Delete it (`DELETE /trades/:id`, which frees the entitlement) and re-exercise instead — see [Corporate actions](#corporate-actions).
- `PUT /trades/:id` and `DELETE /trades/:id` return `422` if the trade is an original parcel anchoring a [rights sale](#selling-or-lapsing-rights) (`rights_sale_allocations.purchase_trade_id`): the sale's record-date anchoring caps were validated against its date and quantity. Delete the rights sale (`DELETE /rights_sales/:id`), make the change, then re-enter it.
- `PUT /trades/:id` returns `422` if the existing trade belongs to a scrip-for-scrip exchange or demerger group (`scrip_action_id` / `demerger_action_id` set): its figures carry the rollover's cost base and deemed acquisition date, which a free-form edit would corrupt. Delete the group (`DELETE /sells/:id` on its closing Sell) and re-exchange/re-demerge instead — see [Corporate actions](#corporate-actions).
- `PUT /trades/:id` and `DELETE /trades/:id` return `422` if the existing trade belongs to a holding-account transfer group (`transfer_id` set, or the crypto network-fee disposal Sell linked via `transfers.fee_sale_trade_id`) — the group only ever changes as a whole, via `DELETE /transfers/:id` (see [Transfers](#transfers)) — or it is an [ESS vest Buy](#ess-statements) (`ess_statement_id` set): edit by deleting the [ESS statement](#ess-statements) and re-vesting.

An unreferenced trade edits and deletes freely.

## Income

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/income` | List all income records |
| `GET` | `/income/:id` | Get one income record |
| `PUT` | `/income/:id` | Create or update an income record |
| `DELETE` | `/income/:id` | Delete an income record |
| `POST` | `/income/:id/reinvest` | Create the DRP reinvestment trade for this distribution (see [DRP reinvestment](#drp-reinvestment)) |
| `DELETE` | `/income/:id/reinvest` | Undo this distribution's reinvestment — delete the DRP trade and clear the link (see [DRP reinvestment](#drp-reinvestment)) |

`PUT /income/:id` and `DELETE /income/:id` return `422` for a buy-back dividend-component row (`buyback_trade_id` set): its figures derive from the buy-back's terms and it belongs with its participation Sell. Delete the Sell via `DELETE /sells/:id` (which removes this row too) and re-participate instead — see [Corporate actions](#corporate-actions).

**`reinvestment_trade_id` is read-only provenance**, managed solely by the reinvest operation: `POST /income/:id/reinvest` sets it, `DELETE /income/:id/reinvest` clears it. `PUT /income/:id` accepts no such field — a value in the body is ignored (a client can't forge a link to an arbitrary trade), and an edit of a reinvested row **preserves** the existing link rather than clearing it. `DELETE /income/:id` on a reinvested row returns `422` (deleting the distribution alone would orphan its DRP trade): undo the reinvestment first, then delete the row.

**While a distribution is reinvested, what the reinvestment used is frozen.** The operation validated the enrolment covering the entitlement date, in this listing and this holding account, and computed the DRP trade's units and residual from this row's cash — so `PUT /income/:id` rejects with `422` (naming the field) a change to `listing_id`, `holding_account_id`, `currency`, `date_paid`, `ex_date`, `entitlement_date`, `trust_income`, or any cash component (`franked_amount`, `unfranked_amount`, `foreign_source_income`, `foreign_tax_paid`, `tfn_withholding_tax`). Left free: the notional and memo figures the reinvestment never read — franking credits, the LIC and CFI amounts, the per-share cross-check pair, `tax_deferred_amount`. Without the freeze an edit reintroduces exactly what the operation refuses — a link across listings, a trade in another account's residual chain, a reinvestment resting on an enrolment that no longer covers it, or a parcel costed from cash that no longer exists. To make such a change: undo the reinvestment (`DELETE /income/:id/reinvest`, or the **Undo reinvest** action), edit, reinvest again.

**`income_type`** (`Dividend` | `EmploymentIncome` | `OtherIncome`, default `Dividend`) — what kind of payment the row records. `Dividend` is a payment **of** the holding (a company dividend, a trust distribution, or a buy-back's dividend component) and is what every row is unless stated otherwise. `EmploymentIncome` is a **dividend equivalent** paid on unvested RSUs: ordinary income as **remuneration** under s 6-5, "not a dividend in the employee's hands", no part of the [ESS discount](#ess-statements), and carrying no franking (TD 2017/26, `docs/ato/ess-dividend-equivalents.md`). `OtherIncome` is ordinary income the holding **produced but did not distribute** — a crypto **staking reward**, or an airdrop of an **established** token, assessable at the tokens' market value when they were received (QC 69950, `docs/ato/crypto-staking-airdrops.md`); the tokens themselves are then entered as a Buy at that same value, opening a parcel with its own 12-month clock.

A row of **either non-dividend kind** carries **the cash and nothing else**: enter the payment as `unfranked_amount` (the row's plain-cash column), and `franked_amount`, `franking_credits`, `foreign_source_income`, `foreign_tax_paid`, `tfn_withholding_tax` (an employer withholds PAYG, which is on your income statement, not the no-TFN withholding this column records), `lic_capital_gain_amount`, `conduit_foreign_income`, `tax_deferred_amount`, `ex_date`, `entitlement_date`, the per-share cross-check pair, and `trust_income` are each rejected `422` naming the field and the kind — every one is a distribution concept. Neither is **reinvestable** (`POST /income/:id/reinvest` → `422`): a DRP reinvests a payment of the holding into more of it, and neither remuneration (paid for services) nor a staking reward (already paid in the tokens) is one.

Where it is reported: the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s own `employment_income` line, which is **informational** — the amount belongs at **item 1/2, salary and wages**, which this system does not otherwise report and which the ATO normally prefills from the employer's STP reporting, so it is added to no dividend line and to no investment-income total, and must not be entered on the return a second time. The [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report) prints it in its own **Employment income** table (never among the dividends, and with no franking status), the [listing activity ledger](#listing-activity) labels it *Employment income (dividend equivalent)*, and the [performance report](#performance) leaves it out of income yield — it is not a return *on* the holding. Kind is part of the [health report](#health)'s duplicate-income key too: a dividend and a dividend equivalent of the same amount on one day are two different payments.

An `OtherIncome` row differs from that one in where it lands, because **nothing prefills item 24**: it reports on the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s own `other_income` line against ATO label **24**, and *is* counted in `gross_assessable_investment_income` — while joining no dividend, franking or trust line. The [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report) prints it in its own **Other income (item 24)** table, the [listing activity ledger](#listing-activity) labels it *Other income (staking reward / airdrop)*, and the [performance report](#performance) **does** count it in income yield: unlike a dividend equivalent, a staking reward is a return the holding itself produced.

**No negative amounts:** every money figure on the row — `franked_amount`, `unfranked_amount`, `foreign_source_income`, `foreign_tax_paid`, `tfn_withholding_tax`, `franking_credits`, `lic_capital_gain_amount`, `conduit_foreign_income`, and the per-share cross-check pair — is the statement's own positive (or zero) amount. A negative value returns `422` naming the field: it would silently reduce the year's totals in every report. (`tax_deferred_amount` carries the same rule; see below.)

**Franking credits are bounded by the dividend behind them:** on a **non-trust** row, `franking_credits` must have a `franked_amount` behind it, and may not exceed the maximum a company could have attached to that amount — `franked_amount × 30/70` at the 30% company tax rate, the largest of the corporate rates for imputation purposes (a base-rate entity's 27.5%/26%/25% gives less, and a partly franked dividend less again). Both breaches return `422`, the second naming the ceiling. A credit above the maximum is not merely improbable: per `docs/ato/allocating-franking-credits.md` (QC 47305), where a statement shows more, "the recipient is only entitled to a franking credit equal to the maximum amount" — so the excess was never claimable, and left standing it inflates a *refundable* offset in the [tax summary](#tax-summary).

Two scope limits. The comparison allows the greater of one cent and **0.5%** over the maximum, absorbing the rounding statements apply to both printed figures — the ATO's own Example 6 (`docs/ato/you-and-your-shares-dividends.md`: $13,066 fully franked carrying $5,600) sits 29 cents above an exact 30/70. And the check does not run on distributions paid before **1 July 2001**, when higher corporate rates (34%, 36%, 39%) allowed proportionally larger credits. **Trust rows are exempt entirely**: a trust's "franked distributions from trusts" component can be reduced by the trust's own deductions while the member still claims the full franking credit (`docs/ato/amma-statement-guidance-notes.md`, Part B item 13Q), so the ratio genuinely need not hold there. (An AMIT row rejects franking credits outright — see AMIT cash distributions below.) The same ceiling binds a [buy-back participation](#corporate-actions)'s dividend component, checked against the row it would create — its per-unit terms times the units tendered.

**Conduit foreign income is a memo inside the unfranked amount:** `conduit_foreign_income` records the part of `unfranked_amount` the payer declared to be **conduit foreign income** (CFI). It is a memo figure — entered **within** the unfranked amount, never in addition to it — and a value exceeding `unfranked_amount` returns `422` naming both figures. A statement that splits its unfranked amount into a CFI line and a non-CFI line is entered with `unfranked_amount` as the **sum** of the two and `conduit_foreign_income` as the CFI line alone.

This is what makes the figure assessable exactly once. CFI is non-assessable non-exempt only for a **foreign resident** member; for the Australian-resident individual this system reports for (the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s `taxpayer_basis`) an unfranked dividend declared to be CFI is ordinary assessable income — the ATO's AMMA guidance notes put it in "Dividends: unfranked amount declared to be CFI", "which forms part of the non-primary production income" (`docs/ato/amma-statement-guidance-notes.md`, Part B item 13U). Because it sits inside `unfranked_amount`, it is counted in `dividends_assessable` and in `gross_assessable_investment_income` through that field, and every report reads `conduit_foreign_income` itself for reference only — adding it again would double-count the same dollars. The [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report) prints it as its own memo column on the dividend and trust-income tables (headed "CFI, within unfranked"), so an entered figure still ties back to the statement's CFI line. AMIT rows carry no CFI at all — see [AMIT cash distributions](#income) below.

**LIC capital gain: enter the advised amount, not the deduction:** `lic_capital_gain_amount` is the **LIC capital gain amount** — the *attributable part* a listed investment company advises on its dividend statement — entered exactly as printed. The deduction is **50%** of it for the resident individual every tax figure here assumes, and the [tax summary](#tax-summary) computes that halving into its `lic_capital_gain_deduction` line at question D8 (the [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report)'s per-dividend `lic_capital_gain_deduction_aud` column is the same halved figure, so the rows sum to the line). The ATO's own example (`docs/ato/lic-capital-gain-deduction.md`): Ben's $70 fully franked dividend carries a $50 attributable part, and he claims **$25** at D8 — so `lic_capital_gain_amount` is `50`, and the report shows `25`. Halving it yourself before entry claims half of what you are entitled to. *(The field took the already-halved figure before 2026-08-17, with nothing saying so; migration `0025` doubles existing rows back to the advised amount, so a database keyed under the old convention reads forward unchanged. The other deduction rates — 33⅓% for a complying superannuation entity or life insurance company — are not modelled; see [Known limitations](#known-limitations).)*

**Per-share cross-check:** a record can optionally carry the statement's per-share figures, `amount_per_security` and `securities_held` (both decimal). They must be supplied together — exactly one present returns `422`. When both are present, the write is validated inside the write transaction: amount_per_security × securities_held, rounded to the cent (half away from zero, matching statements), must equal the gross cash components `franked_amount + unfranked_amount + foreign_source_income` (franking credits are notional and TFN withholding is deducted from — not part of — the gross); a mismatch returns `422` with the computed product in the body. Omitting both skips the check. The stored values are validation/cross-reference only — no report or calculation uses them.

**Entitlement date (trust distributions):** a `trust_income` record can optionally carry `entitlement_date` — the date the holder became presently entitled, usually the distribution period's end printed on the statement. Trust income is assessed in the income year of **present entitlement** regardless of when the cash is paid (ATO QC 23087, `docs/ato/trust-income-timing.md`), so a June distribution paid in mid-July belongs to the FY just ended: when the date is set, the [tax summary](#tax-summary) attributes **every** component of the row (the financial-year bucket and the AUD-conversion month) by it instead of `date_paid`. Absent, `date_paid` behaviour is unchanged. A dividend is assessed when paid or credited, so supplying `entitlement_date` on a non-trust row returns `422` (also CHECK-enforced in the schema). The franking 45-day at-risk test anchors on when the units went **ex**, not on when the income is assessed: the recorded `ex_date`, else — on a trust row — `entitlement_date`, since a distribution's units go ex at the end of the period that date records, else `date_paid`. Anchoring a trust distribution on payment alone made the test unreliable in one direction only: units sold between the period end and the payment were never entitled in the walk, so credits the holding-period rule denies were claimed in full. A dividend with neither date recorded still falls back to `date_paid` and is listed by the [franking at-risk report](#franking-at-risk) as `untested_no_ex_date` rather than passing quietly. The A$5,000 small-shareholder threshold year follows the row's assessment year.

**Tax-deferred amount (non-AMIT trust distributions):** a `trust_income` record can optionally carry `tax_deferred_amount` (decimal, ≥ 0) — the statement's tax-deferred amount, which for a non-AMIT unit trust is a CGT event E4 cost-base reduction (`docs/ato/cgt-non-assessable-payments.md`). The field is **informational**: no calculation reads it, and recording it changes nothing by itself — the reduction is entered as a `ReturnOfCapital` [corporate action](#corporate-actions) on the listing, exactly as before. Its purpose is the [E4 cross-check report](#tax-deferred-e4-cross-check), which flags every row whose non-zero amount has no same-FY action, so a faithfully keyed statement can't silently leave the cost base overstated. Supplying it on a non-trust row returns `422` (a company's non-assessable payment is entered as the corporate action directly; also CHECK-enforced in the schema), as does a negative value.

**AMIT cash distributions (cash-only rows):** an income row on a listing that is an AMIT **for that row's financial year** (its `amit` flag, from [`amit_from`](#listings) where the fund converted) records the fund's **cash distribution advice only**. It drives the cash machinery exactly like any other row — [DRP reinvestment](#drp-reinvestment) (the gross cash less the amounts withheld at source), the per-share cross-check, the ex-date enrolment check — but contributes **nothing** to the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s income lines: for an AMIT the AMMA statement's attribution is the only assessable record (the cash advice is not a tax document), so counting the cash alongside the [AMMA components](#amma-statements) would double the year's income. The exclusion is whole-row (cash, withholding, offsets). Write-time validation keeps these rows cash-only: `PUT /income/:id` on a row in one of the listing's AMIT years returns `422` unless `trust_income` is set (an AMIT is an attribution managed investment *trust*), for a non-zero notional component (`franking_credits`, `lic_capital_gain_amount`, `conduit_foreign_income` — the fund's attribution belongs on its AMMA statement, where the tax summary reads it), and for a `tax_deferred_amount` (an AMIT's cost-base movement is the AMMA `cost_base_adjustment`, entered as [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments) — CGT event E10, not E4). The cash components (`franked_amount`, `unfranked_amount`, `foreign_source_income`) and the source-withholding fields (`foreign_tax_paid`, `tfn_withholding_tax` — they reduce the DRP-reinvestable cash) stay recordable. The [AMIT cash cross-check report](#amit-cash-cross-check) flags any year whose cash rows have no covering AMMA statement, so cash-only entry can't silently drop a year's income from the return.

## Interest income

Interest income (`docs/ato/tax-return-labels-2026.md`): bank, term-deposit, or broker-cash interest. Interest has no listing, so it is its own entity rather than an [income](#income) record. The [tax summary](#tax-summary) reports an **Australian-source** row's gross as its `interest_income` line (question 10, label 10L), joining the TFN amount withheld to the combined withholding line (10M); a **foreign-source** row (interest-like income from a foreign payer — e.g. a US broker's Treasury liquidity / money-market sweep fund) instead reports as assessable foreign source income on the `foreign_interest_income` line (question 20, label 20E), with its foreign tax withheld joining the FITO line (20O, subject to the A$1,000 de-minimis). Both classifications count in gross assessable investment income.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/interest_income` | List all interest income records |
| `GET` | `/interest_income/:id` | Get one interest income record |
| `PUT` | `/interest_income/:id` | Create or update an interest income record |
| `DELETE` | `/interest_income/:id` | Delete an interest income record |

**`date_paid` is the date credited:** interest is declared "in the year it is credited, received or applied or dealt with in any way on your behalf or as you direct. For term deposits this usually means you should declare interest in the year the investment matures" (ATO *Investment income*, QC 72101, `docs/ato/investment-income-timing.md`). So the date to key is the statement's **credit** date, never the date the money became reachable: a term deposit crediting $500 on 30 June with the funds only withdrawable on 2 July is FY2026 interest, not FY2027. A deposit run to maturity with no interim credits is credited at maturity (and a rollover or automatic reinvestment is a credit at the rollover date, of the amount that would have been received); a longer deposit paying interest away to another account periodically is credited at each of those payment dates, so each is its own row. There is deliberately **no second date column** for when the funds became available — unlike a trust distribution's `entitlement_date` (see [Income](#income)), which exists because present entitlement and payment are two different tax facts. Availability is not a tax fact at all, and a column for it would only invite keying it as the assessment date.

Fields: `date_paid` (the date credited, above — its month sets the financial year and the ATO FX conversion month), `amount` (the **gross** interest including any amount withheld — the gross figure is declared), `tfn_withholding_tax` (the TFN amount withheld, 10M — Australian-source rows only), `foreign_source` (boolean, default false: whether the payer is foreign, routing the row to 20E instead of 10L), `foreign_tax_paid` (foreign tax withheld from the gross amount, joining the FITO line — foreign-source rows only), `currency` (defaults to AUD), an optional free-text `source` description (e.g. the bank account — informational only), and an optional `holding_account_id` link for interest paid on a portfolio account such as a broker cash account (NULL for an ordinary bank account; informational only). `PUT` returns `422` for an unrecognised `currency` or `holding_account_id`, a negative `amount`, `tfn_withholding_tax`, or `foreign_tax_paid` (interest figures are the statement's positive — or zero — amounts), a `foreign_tax_paid` on an Australian-source row (foreign tax needs a foreign payer, or the FITO line would claim an offset the row can't support), or a `tfn_withholding_tax` on a foreign-source row (TFN amounts are withheld by Australian investment bodies).

## Investment expenses

Deductible investment expenses (`docs/ato/investment-income-deductions.md`, `docs/ato/dividend-income-deductions.md`): the cost of earning assessable investment income — interest on money borrowed to buy income-producing shares, management/adviser fees, account-keeping fees, and subscriptions. The [tax summary](#tax-summary) nets these against gross assessable investment income per Australian financial year.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/investment_expenses` | List all investment expenses |
| `GET` | `/investment_expenses/:id` | Get one investment expense |
| `PUT` | `/investment_expenses/:id` | Create or update an investment expense |
| `DELETE` | `/investment_expenses/:id` | Delete an investment expense |

Fields: `date_incurred` (its month sets the financial year and the ATO FX conversion month), `expense_type` (`LoanInterest` | `ManagementFee` | `AdviceFee` | `AccountKeepingFee` | `Subscription` | `Other`), `amount` (the **deductible amount** — post-apportionment, the figure that goes on the return and the value the tax summary totals), the optional provenance `gross_amount` and `deductible_percentage` (see the apportionment cross-check below — no calculation reads them), `currency` (defaults to AUD), an optional `description`, and the optional `listing_id` / `holding_account_id` links (both nullable — leave blank for a portfolio-wide expense). `PUT` returns `422` for an unrecognised `currency`, `listing_id`, or `holding_account_id`, or an `expense_type` outside the enum set. Apportionment (joint accounts, private vs income-producing use) is the user's determination, not computed here; brokerage is not recorded here (it forms a trade's CGT cost base) and the LIC capital gain deduction is its own income field.

**No negative amounts, and a percentage is a percentage:** `amount` and `gross_amount` are the invoice's own positive (or zero) figures — a negative value returns `422` naming the field. A negative deduction is arithmetically income: it reduces the year's deduction total, and because the [tax summary](#tax-summary) subtracts that total from gross assessable investment income, a `-500` expense lifts `net_assessable_investment_income` **above** `gross_assessable_investment_income`. `deductible_percentage` must be within `0`–`100` inclusive; outside that it returns `422` naming the rejected value.

**One row is one financial year — a multi-year expense is entered per year:** `date_incurred` puts the **whole** `amount` in that date's financial year, which is right for a fee, a subscription, a month's loan interest, and any prepayment inside the 12-month rule. Two ordinary share-investor expenses are apportioned across years instead and have to be keyed accordingly (`docs/ato/expense-time-apportionment.md`): **borrowing expenses** — loan establishment fees, legal expenses, stamp duty on the loan — totalling more than $100 are spread "over 5 years or the loan term, whichever is shorter" (ATO *Dividend income deductions*, QC 104069; s 25-25 — $100 or less is deductible in full in the year incurred); and a **prepaid expense** whose eligible service period runs more than 12 months, or ends after the last day of the next income year, is apportioned by days, `amount × (days of the service period in the year ÷ total days)`, over the lesser of the service period and 10 years (*Deductions for prepaid expenses*, QC 106556). Neither is computed here: enter **one row per financial year** carrying that year's share, with the `description` naming the whole expense and its position in the sequence ("loan establishment fee, year 2 of 5"). A $2,000 establishment fee keyed as one row claims five years' deduction at once and nothing refuses it — the apportionment, like the private-use percentage beside it, is the taxpayer's own working. See [Known limitations](#known-limitations).

**Apportionment cross-check:** when **both** `gross_amount` and `deductible_percentage` are supplied, they must reconcile with what is claimed — `gross_amount × deductible_percentage`, rounded to the cent (half away from zero), must equal `amount` to the cent; a mismatch returns `422` with the computed figure in the body. Both sides are cent-rounded because either may carry sub-cent precision (a fee stated to more decimals, a percentage that doesn't divide evenly) while the money on the return is cents. Supplying **one** of the pair, or neither, skips the check — either alone records less than a determination, so there is nothing to reconcile. The same shape as the [income](#income) per-share cross-check: the fields exist to record how the deductible figure was arrived at, and a triple that contradicts itself (a 50% share claimed at the gross figure) is an over-claim the row itself already contains the evidence for.

## AMMA statements

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/amma_statements` | List all AMMA statements |
| `GET` | `/amma_statements/:id` | Get one AMMA statement |
| `PUT` | `/amma_statements/:id` | Create or update an AMMA statement |
| `DELETE` | `/amma_statements/:id` | Delete an AMMA statement |
| `POST` | `/amma_statements/:id/generate_adjustments` | Generate this statement's per-parcel [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments) (see [Generating AMIT adjustments](#generating-amit-adjustments)) |

`tax_year_end_date` must be a **30 June** date (any year): an AMMA statement attributes a full Australian financial year, and every report that reads it — the [tax summary](#tax-summary), [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain), and franking reports — buckets the statement into the FY named by that date's calendar year. `PUT` returns `422` for any other date, naming the rejected date. The statement's **`currency` must be its listing's** (`422` naming both otherwise): it attributes *that fund's* own year, and the [tax summary](#tax-summary) converts every component at this currency's rate for the month of `tax_year_end_date` — a statement in another currency would report the fund's year in money the fund does not pay. Convert a foreign statement before entry, as the [trade](#trades) and [ESS statement](#ess-statements) paths require.

**The two foreign-tax lines.** Part C reports foreign income tax offsets on foreign *income* and on foreign *capital gains* separately, because they are claimed differently, and this is the one place the distinction has to be entered by hand: `foreign_tax_credits` is the income line, claimable in full; `foreign_tax_credits_capital_gains` is the capital-gains line, which the [tax summary](#tax-summary) **apportions to the assessable part of those gains** before claiming it (the Division 115 discount halves it — `docs/ato/fito-capital-gains-apportionment.md`). Enter each exactly as the statement prints it: trustees report the capital-gains figure grossed up on purpose, since the reduction is the member's step. The field defaults to `0`, so a statement whose Part C gives one combined figure behaves exactly as before until its capital-gains portion is moved across. `PUT` returns `422` for a `foreign_tax_credits_capital_gains` stated with **no** capital gains on the statement — there would be no proportion to apportion it by, so the whole figure would be claimed in full, which is what the split exists to prevent.

`DELETE` returns `422` while the statement's [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments) are still stored, naming how many there are (see [Deletes blocked by a dependant](#deletes-blocked-by-a-dependant)) — generating them (below) is what usually creates them. There is no cascade: each adjustment is removed individually with `DELETE /amit_adjustments/:id`, and the statement becomes deletable once the last one is gone. (To *replace* a statement's adjustments rather than remove them, re-run generation with `"replace": true` instead — it clears and regenerates them in one request.)

### Generating AMIT adjustments

```
POST /amma_statements/:id/generate_adjustments
{"replace": false, "preview": false}
```

Creates one [AMIT adjustment](#amit-adjustments) per parcel **open at the statement's `tax_year_end_date`**, restricted to the statement's own `listing_id` and `holding_account_id`, so the statement's per-unit `cost_base_adjustment` reaches every affected parcel without 30 rows being typed by hand. The parcels come from the same open-holdings read every open-parcel report uses, and each row is written through the ordinary AMIT-adjustment write path — same per-row invariants, same [row history](#row-history) audit trail — inside **one transaction**, so a partial set can never persist. Both body fields default to `false`; an absent or empty body is the same as `{}`.

Answers `201 Created` with `{"created": [...], "units_adjusted", "units_held", "difference", "preview": false}` (plus `unattributed` when a rollover left units for hand entry — see below) — `created` is the adjustment rows, `units_adjusted` their total **re-based into the statement year's unit basis** (the stored quantities are in each parcel's as-acquired units), and `difference` is `units_adjusted − units_held`. A non-zero difference **does not block the write**: it is a reconciliation, not an invariant (a statement may state units at a date other than year end). It is surfaced here and the statement stays flagged by the [AMIT adjustment cross-check](#amit-adjustment-cross-check) until it is resolved.

`"preview": true` computes the same result and writes nothing, answering `200 OK` with `"preview": true` and the rows it *would* create (carrying the ids they would take). Every refusal below still applies, so a preview answers the same `422` the write would — it is the web UI's confirm step, not a weaker check.

Returns `404` naming the statement when it does not exist, and `422` when:

- the statement **already has adjustments** and `replace` is not set (with `"replace": true` the existing rows are deleted and regenerated in the same transaction — the usual repair after entering a trade that was missing);
- **no parcels** of the statement's listing were open in its holding account at the year end — generation derives its set from the parcels open at that date, so there is nothing to write, and an empty set would hide the different situations that reach here. The refusal names them: the position's trades have not been entered yet (enter them and run it again); the holding was **sold during the year**, in which case the statement still adjusts the units it covered and those rows are [entered by hand](#amit-adjustments) against the parcels those units came from — one row per parcel, `quantity` set to the units it held (the normal path for the year of a sale, whose statement arrives months afterwards stating nil units held, and the [cross-check](#amit-adjustment-cross-check) reconciles such a set rather than flagging it); or it was **transferred, exchanged or demerged away during the year**, in which case the rows go against the **replacement parcels** that now hold those units, which the write path accepts because the units trace back to this account.

**A rollover after the year end is followed, not refused.** A parcel open at the statement's year end whose units a [transfer](#transfers) has carried away since — the ordinary case, since the statement arrives months after 30 June — has its row written **against the transfer-in parcel**, wherever the transfer moved it: the replacement carries exactly the units moved under the source parcel's own acquisition date, so it is identified without guessing, and that parcel is where those units' cost base now lives. (Before this, the run was refused and the statement's reduction could be recorded nowhere at all — the source parcel refuses it because the units are gone, and the replacement used to be refused by the per-account rule.) A **scrip-for-scrip exchange or demerger** is not attributed for you: its replacement quantities are ratio-scaled and nothing links a replacement to the individual parcel it replaced, so those units are returned in the response's `unattributed` list — `[{ "source_trade_id", "units", "replacements" }]`, omitted when empty — to be entered by hand against the named replacement parcel, which is likewise accepted. Units that stayed behind (a partial move) still get their own row against the source parcel, so one parcel can produce two rows.

A **share split** between the covered parcels' acquisition dates and the year end — including one that falls between two covered parcels, leaving them on different unit bases — generates normally: each parcel's quantity is stored in its own as-acquired basis and re-based into the statement year's basis when the reduction is computed (see [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments)).

**Which parcels the per-unit figure reaches.** `cost_base_adjustment` is applied **uniformly to every unit held at the statement's `tax_year_end_date`** — that is the whole rule, and the `units_adjusted` vs `units_held` reconciliation depends on it. Two consequences are worth stating. First, a parcel bought *after* the fund's last distribution period of the year is covered at the same per-unit figure as one held all year, even though it drew no distribution: the registry's `units_held` at 30 June counts it too, so the year's total cost-base movement comes out right while its split between parcels is an apportionment this tool makes rather than one the fund states. Second, a statement that gives a **total** AMIT cost base net amount for the year rather than a per-unit figure is entered by dividing that total over the units the statement covers — Σ then reconciles by construction. A member who needs a different apportionment (for instance one that gives a late parcel nothing) enters the rows by hand instead of generating them: `quantity` decides which units each row reaches, per [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments) above. The ATO states the AMIT cost base net amount as an annual, member-level amount applied to the member's units, without prescribing how it is spread across parcels acquired at different times (`docs/ato/amit-cost-base-adjustments.md`).

## AMIT adjustments

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/amit_adjustments` | List all AMIT adjustments |
| `GET` | `/amit_adjustments/:id` | Get one AMIT adjustment |
| `PUT` | `/amit_adjustments/:id` | Create or update an AMIT adjustment |
| `DELETE` | `/amit_adjustments/:id` | Delete an AMIT adjustment |

Returns `422 Unprocessable Entity` if the referenced trade is not a Buy/DRP, the trade and AMMA statement reference different listings **or different holding accounts** (a registry issues one statement per holder account, so a statement only adjusts its own account's parcels — with one exception: a **rollover replacement parcel** whose units trace back to the statement's own account through a [transfer](#transfers), [scrip-for-scrip exchange](#exchanging-a-scrip-for-scrip-takeover) or [demerger](#demerging) *is* accepted, wherever the operation moved them, because that parcel is where those units' cost base now lives; the chain is followed, so a holding moved twice is still reachable, and a parcel that never held this account's units is refused as before), the quantity exceeds the trade quantity, **another row already adjusts the same parcel on the same statement** — applying the statement's per-unit figure to one parcel twice reduces its cost base twice, and CGT event E10's nil floor can turn that over-reduction into a capital gain that was never made. (Also enforced by a UNIQUE index; see `docs/SCHEMA.md`.) A row is also refused when a **transfer, scrip-for-scrip exchange or demerger has already carried the parcel's units into a replacement parcel**: those operations compute the replacement's cost base when they run and store it as a fixed figure, so a reduction entered against the original afterwards reaches nothing — the original is fully consumed, and its closing Sell is not a disposal, so no report nets the amount off anything. The refusal names the replacement parcel(s) and how many of the original's units (if any) a rollover has *not* taken, and it names the way round: enter the rest **against the replacement parcel**, where those units now are — accepted for a statement of the account they came from (see above) — which is exactly what [generating the statement's set](#generating-amit-adjustments) does for you. An ordinary Sell, a buy-back participation and a worthless recognise are **real disposals** whose gain the reduction does reach, and are never refused — a statement for the year a holding was sold is entered exactly this way (see [Generating AMIT adjustments](#generating-amit-adjustments)).

`quantity` is in the parcel's **as-acquired** units — the basis the trade's own `quantity` is in, and the one that caps it. The statement's `cost_base_adjustment`, by contrast, is per unit *as the statement's tax year saw them*. Where a [share split/consolidation or bonus issue](#corporate-actions) falls between the parcel's acquisition and the statement's `tax_year_end_date` the two are on different bases, so the reduction each row applies is **`quantity` re-based into the year-end basis × `cost_base_adjustment`** — enter the fund's per-unit figure exactly as stated and the parcel's units exactly as transacted; neither needs scaling by hand. (A 100-unit parcel that a 2-for-1 split has made 200 units by the year end is reduced by 200 × the per-unit figure.) The same re-basing drives the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) report's CGT event E10 excess.

`quantity` also decides **which** units of the parcel the reduction reaches, which matters once part of the parcel has been sold. A row covering the whole parcel reduces every unit by the statement's per-unit figure, including units disposed of during the year — the fund attributing to units held *during* the year, which s 104-107B makes an adjustment "just before the end of the income year, **or just before the time of a relevant CGT event**" (LCR 2015/11 para 13). A row covering **less** than the whole parcel covers the units still held at the statement's `tax_year_end_date` first — which is what [Generating AMIT adjustments](#generating-amit-adjustments) writes, one row per parcel for the units it still had open — and only spills onto the units sold earlier once it covers more than those. Either way the row takes off exactly `quantity` × the per-unit figure in total; the coverage decides only how that total is split between the units sold (netted off their cost base in the [realised gains](#realised-gains) report) and the units still held (the [open parcels](#open-parcels) report's `amit_cost_base_reduction`, which is the reduction reaching the *remaining* units, not the whole parcel's). If the units a row covers are reduced past nil, the excess is a CGT event E10 capital gain in the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) report, measured against those units' cost base.

Whole sets are usually better generated than typed: see [Generating AMIT adjustments](#generating-amit-adjustments), and the [AMIT adjustment cross-check](#amit-adjustment-cross-check) for whether an existing set reconciles to its statement.

**This is an AMIT's only cost-base movement.** The E10 reduction these rows carry and the E4 reduction a [`ReturnOfCapital`](#corporate-actions) carries are **mutually exclusive paths**, not two views of one figure: nothing relates them, so the same money entered both ways reduces the parcel twice and no cross-check sees it. Both other doors are therefore shut on a listing flagged [`amit`](#listings) — a `tax_deferred_amount` on its [income](#income) rows and a `ReturnOfCapital` on the listing are each refused `422` pointing back at the statement's `cost_base_adjustment`. (A fund that converted to an AMIT records [`amit_from`](#listings), and keeps the payments made while it was an ordinary trust; the cost-base chain nets both kinds against one running balance — see [Net capital gain](#net-capital-gain).)

## ESS statements

The income side of an employee share scheme interest (`docs/ato/employee-share-schemes.md`): one row per Employee share scheme statement, carrying the Item 12 discount labels declared in the year of the taxing point. The assessable discount reaches the [tax summary](#tax-summary); the [Vesting](#vesting-an-ess-statement) operation creates the cost-base-reset Buy for the vested shares.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/ess_statements` | List all ESS statements |
| `GET` | `/ess_statements/:id` | Get one ESS statement |
| `PUT` | `/ess_statements/:id` | Create or update an ESS statement |
| `DELETE` | `/ess_statements/:id` | Delete an ESS statement (and its vest Buy, if any) |
| `POST` | `/ess_statements/:id/vest` | Create the cost-base-reset Buy for this statement (see [Vesting](#vesting-an-ess-statement)) |

Fields: `listing_id`, `holding_account_id` (defaults to the seeded default account), `taxing_point_date`, `quantity` and `market_value_per_share` (the vested shares and their per-share market value — the vest Buy's quantity and price), the discount labels `taxed_upfront_eligible` (D), `taxed_upfront_not_eligible` (E), `deferral_discount` (F), `pre_2009_cessation_discount` (G), the `foreign_source_discount` memo (A — already within the above, surfaced for the FITO calc), `tfn_withholding` (C), `currency` (defaults to AUD), and the optional `fx_rate`. An unrecognised currency/listing/account is rejected `422`, as is a `currency` that is not the **listing's own** — `market_value_per_share` is the market value of that listed share, so the per-share value and the listed price are the same money (the rule the [DRP reinvest](#drp-reinvestment) path applies to a distribution's cash); convert the employer's statement before entry, or choose the listing quoted in its currency. The refusal names both currencies.

**What a statement may say** is checked at write time — the figures reach the [tax summary](#tax-summary) and the [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report) as entered, so a contradiction between them is refused `422` rather than reported:

- **No negative amount.** An employer's statement reports positive (or zero) figures. A negative discount label nets against the year's other statements, a negative `tfn_withholding` is withholding refunded from nowhere, and a negative `quantity`/`market_value_per_share` is not a parcel. The refusal names the field (the statement-AUD overrides are covered too).
- **A taxing point on or after 20 September 1985.** The [vest](#vesting-an-ess-statement) Buy is dated the taxing point, and `PUT /trades` refuses a pre-CGT trade (a pre-CGT holding is outside CGT and not modelled) — so the statement is refused at the earlier place. No ESS interest can predate CGT in any case: Division 83A dates from 2009 and its predecessor from 1995, so this catches a typo.
- **A taxing point no later than today.** Same reasoning at the other end: the [vest](#vesting-an-ess-statement) Buy is dated the taxing point and `PUT /trades` refuses a trade dated after today, so the statement is refused at the earlier place. An employer's statement reports a taxing point that has already happened, so a future one is a typo. Today itself is accepted.
- **`foreign_source_discount` (label A) no larger than D + E + F + G.** It is the foreign-sourced *portion* of those labels — a memo recorded within them, never added on top — so a memo larger than what it is a memo of is a contradiction (the same rule [income](#income) applies to `conduit_foreign_income` within `unfranked_amount`). Equality is ordinary (a wholly foreign-sourced discount). The same check applies to `aud_foreign_source_discount` against the other statement-AUD overrides, but only where that total is *knowable* — a label carrying an amount with no override converts at the RBA rate, which no write-time check can resolve, so such a statement is left alone rather than guessed at.
- **The discount labels no larger than `quantity × market_value_per_share`**, when both of those are positive. The discount *is* the market value less what the employee paid, so a larger discount implies a negative payment — the shape a transposed column, or a foreign-currency discount against an AUD market value, makes. Exact equality is the RSU case (nil consideration) and is accepted; both sides are compared cent-rounded. An income-only statement (no vest recorded, so `quantity` and `market_value_per_share` are zero) is exempt.

**`fx_rate`** is the foreign-per-AUD rate the taxpayer states for the statement (`AUD = foreign / rate`, the same convention as a [trade's](#trades) `fx_rate`) — typically the release-date rate the employer's statement converted at. It is a **fallback**: an imported [RBA monthly rate](#rba-fx-rates) for the taxing point's month still wins wherever the statement converts. Where the month has no imported rate, it is what both sides use — the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s discount labels and the vest parcel's cost base — so the income and CGT sides of one statement can never answer off different rates. Only accepted on a non-AUD statement, and must be positive (`422` otherwise). Leaving it unset is normal for a statement whose month is imported; it is the [vest](#vesting-an-ess-statement) that refuses when neither rate exists. Responses additionally carry the read-only `vest_trade_id` — the id of the statement's vest Buy, `null` while unvested (derived from the Buy's back-link, ignored if supplied on `PUT`) — which is how the web UI offers the Vest action only on unvested rows.

**Statement-AUD overrides:** a non-AUD statement can additionally carry the employer statement's stated **AUD** figure for each discount label — `aud_taxed_upfront_eligible`, `aud_taxed_upfront_not_eligible`, `aud_deferral_discount`, `aud_pre_2009_cessation_discount`, `aud_foreign_source_discount` (all optional). Employer statements convert at the **release-date spot rate**, while the [tax summary](#tax-summary) otherwise converts foreign-currency amounts at the **RBA monthly rate** — the figures differ, and the ATO-prefilled return carries the employer's. When an override is present the tax summary reports it **verbatim** for that label (including the $1,000 taxed-upfront reduction calculation); absent, the label converts via the RBA rate as before. Supplying an override on an AUD-denominated statement is rejected `422` (its labels are already the AUD figures, and a second figure could silently disagree).

`PUT /ess_statements/:id` returns `422` once the statement has been vested (a vest Buy carries its `ess_statement_id`) **and the edit changes a field the Buy was created from** — `listing_id`, `holding_account_id`, `taxing_point_date`, `quantity`, `market_value_per_share`, `currency`, or `fx_rate` — which would desync it; delete the statement (which removes the vest) and re-enter to change those. The income-side fields (the discount labels, `tfn_withholding`, the statement-AUD overrides) stay editable after vesting, since the employer's annual ESS statement arrives after each vest is recorded. `DELETE /ess_statements/:id` removes the statement and its vest Buy together, returning `422` while that Buy is **drawn on** by a Sell allocation or AMIT adjustment (remove those first).

### Vesting an ESS statement

```
POST /ess_statements/6/vest
```

Creates the cost-base-reset **Buy** for the vested shares and links it back (`trades.ess_statement_id`) in one transaction — no request body. At the taxing point the ESS interest's first-element cost base is reset to its market value and it is taken to be re-acquired on that date for CGT, so the Buy is dated (and settled) on `taxing_point_date`, with `quantity` shares at `average_price` = `market_value_per_share`, zero brokerage, in the statement's currency. The discount clock runs from the taxing point (no `deemed_acquisition_date`). The income side (the assessable discount) is already on the statement and reaches the tax summary directly.

A **non-AUD** statement's parcel needs a rate to be costed in AUD, and the Buy's `fx_rate` is the *fallback* one — the rate applied when no monthly ATO rate exists for the month — so it is never invented: the statement's stated [`fx_rate`](#ess-statements) is carried onto the Buy when there is one, otherwise the taxing-point month's imported ATO rate is resolved and carried. A statement with **neither** is refused `422` naming the currency and month, rather than binding a placeholder `1` that would cost a US$15,000 parcel at A$15,000 exactly where the rate is missing. Import that month's [RBA rates](#rba-fx-rates), or record the rate the employer used on the statement, and vest again. (An AUD statement carries `1`, which is what an AUD conversion resolves to anyway.)

Returns `201 Created` with the created Buy as JSON, `404 Not Found` if no statement has that id, or `422 Unprocessable Entity` if the statement was already vested (delete it first to redo), its `quantity`/`market_value_per_share` is not positive (nothing to create), or its currency has no rate for the taxing point's month (above). The created Buy is immutable via `PUT /trades/:id` and never deleted individually (`DELETE /trades/:id` → `422`) — `DELETE /ess_statements/:id` removes it.

## Attachments

Supporting documents (a trade confirmation / contract note PDF, a dividend statement, an AMMA statement scan, an annual employee-share-scheme statement, a plain-text exchange record, a demerger booklet or scrip-exchange offer document) attached to exactly one activity — a Trade, an Income record, an AMMA Statement, an ESS Statement, an Interest Income record, or a Corporate Action. The file bytes are stored in the database (a BLOB), so the weekly DB backup captures the documents with no separate file store. Because the payload is binary, these endpoints depart from the JSON-CRUD convention used elsewhere: upload is `multipart/form-data`, list/get return metadata only, and a dedicated endpoint streams the raw content.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/attachments` | List attachment metadata (never the blob); filter by owner with `?trade_id=`, `?income_id=`, `?amma_statement_id=`, `?ess_statement_id=`, `?interest_income_id=`, or `?corporate_action_id=`; a trade filter can add `&include_linked=true` to also return the linked source record's documents (below) |
| `GET` | `/attachments/:id` | Get one attachment's metadata |
| `GET` | `/attachments/:id/content` | Stream the raw file bytes (stored `Content-Type` + `Content-Disposition` filename); `?disposition=inline` renders in place instead of downloading (see below) |
| `POST` | `/attachments` | Upload a file (`multipart/form-data`) |
| `DELETE` | `/attachments/:id` | Delete one attachment |

**Linked documents:** a provenance-created trade's paperwork usually lives on its source record — one registry advice documents both a distribution and its reinvestment, so it is attached to the income row the Reinvest action was run from, and the DRP trade's own attachment list would otherwise always be empty. `GET /attachments?trade_id=…&include_linked=true` therefore also returns the attachments owned by the record the trade was created from, traversing the provenance link at read time: the funding distribution of a DRP trade (`income.reinvestment_trade_id`), the dividend-component income row of a buy-back participation Sell (`income.buyback_trade_id`), and the annual statement of an ESS vest Buy (`trades.ess_statement_id`). Transfers and inheritances trace to records that cannot own attachments at all, so there is nothing to traverse for them. Rights exercises, scrip exchanges, demergers, and worthless-shares sells trace to a corporate action row, which can own attachments directly — but one action can spawn many trades over time (e.g. several exercise Buys against one RightsIssue), so its paperwork is reached via the Corporate Actions screen's own Attachments action rather than a per-trade linked view. Ownership is unchanged — each returned row still carries its true owner's id field (a linked row has `income_id`/`ess_statement_id` set and `trade_id` null), uploads and deletes stay with the owning record, and the web UI labels linked rows and routes Delete to the owner's own Attachments view. `include_linked=true` with no `trade_id`, or combined with another owner filter, returns `422 Unprocessable Entity`.

`POST /attachments` takes a `multipart/form-data` body with the file in a `file` part and **exactly one** owner field — `trade_id`, `income_id`, `amma_statement_id`, `ess_statement_id`, `interest_income_id`, or `corporate_action_id`. The server computes `byte_size` and the SHA-256 `checksum`, and returns `201 Created` with the stored metadata as JSON. It returns `422 Unprocessable Entity` if no owner or more than one owner is given, the owner id doesn't reference an existing activity, the `file` part is missing, or its content type is outside the allowlist (`application/pdf`, `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `text/plain` — the last so plain-text records like crypto exchange exports and registry DRP advices are attachable); and `413 Payload Too Large` if the file exceeds 25 MB. Deleting the owning Trade / Income record / AMMA Statement / ESS Statement / Interest Income record / Corporate Action removes its attachments automatically (`ON DELETE CASCADE`).

`GET /attachments/:id/content?disposition=inline` sets `Content-Disposition: inline` instead of the default `attachment`, so a browser renders the file (e.g. a PDF or image) in place rather than downloading it — used by the [Attachments report](#attachments-index)'s View link, which opens it in a new tab. `?disposition=attachment` is the explicit form of the default; any other value returns `422 Unprocessable Entity`. Both forms carry `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, pinning the browser to the stored content type rather than sniffing the bytes.

## DRP enrolments

Records when each holding reinvests its distributions, as **dated enrolment periods**: `enrolment_date` (inclusive) to `unenrolment_date` (exclusive; omitted = open-ended, i.e. currently enrolled). A holding can start unenrolled, enrol, unenrol, and re-enrol — one row per period, each with its own residual handling. Enrolment is per **(listing, holding account)**: the same listing may be enrolled in one account and not another (e.g. an employer share-plan account that cannot DRP alongside an enrolled personal account); `holding_account_id` defaults to the seeded default account when omitted.

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `GET` | `/drp_enrolments` | List all enrolment periods |
| `GET` | `/drp_enrolments/:id` | Get one enrolment period |
| `PUT` | `/drp_enrolments/:id` | Create or update an enrolment period |
| `DELETE` | `/drp_enrolments/:id` | Remove an enrolment period (refused once it covers a reinvestment) |

```
PUT /drp_enrolments/1
{ "listing_id": 1, "enrolment_date": "2024-01-01", "unenrolment_date": "2025-01-01",
```
