4. if none is available the report fails loudly rather than leaving an amount unconverted — `422` naming the currency and the month, and pointing at the [import](#rba-fx-rates) that closes the gap (a missing rate is imported reference data the user can supply, not an internal fault; the same answer [report-snapshot generation](#report-snapshots) gives for the same gap).

The monthly rate is the ATO-published convenience default: ATO guidance (`docs/ato/forex-average-rates.md`, QC 18020) permits an average rate only where it is a **reasonable approximation of the spot rates** at the statutory translation times — fine for recurring or small amounts, but its Examples 5 and 7 state an average rate is **not appropriate for a one-off purchase or sale of a large capital asset**. Such a trade should carry the transaction-date spot rate in `spot_fx_rate` (`docs/ato/forex-common-transactions.md`: Lisa translates each leg at the day's rate). Entering the override is deliberate — absent it, behaviour is unchanged (monthly rate first, `fx_rate` fallback), and the silent fallback semantics of existing `fx_rate` values never flip.

Cost base and proceeds in the portfolio, unrealised, and realised reports are converted this way (a rollover/transfer replacement parcel carries its consumed parcel's `fx_rate` *and* `spot_fx_rate`, so the carried AUD cost base is unchanged by the substitution). A parcel's cost-base breakdown converts as one unit at the acquisition-month rate — including the AMIT/return-of-capital reductions that arose in later rate months (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)). Income and AMMA amounts are also converted in the tax summary, using each record's `currency` and the month of `date_paid` (income) or `tax_year_end_date` (AMMA); these records have no manual `fx_rate` or spot override, so a non-AUD amount with no ATO rate fails loudly (`422`, naming the currency and month) rather than being passed through unconverted.

**Valuation-only fallback:** the RBA publishes a month's rate only after the month ends, so *valuing* a holding in the current month (a [report snapshot](#report-snapshots), a [live quote](#live-valuation)) would otherwise be blocked all month. Exactly those two valuation paths may fall back to the most recent earlier month's rate — at most 2 months back, beyond which they fail loudly as above — and the result is always flagged (the snapshot's `provisional` flag, the row's `fx_provisional`), never silently substituted. No tax calculation or FY report can reach a fallback-month rate: cost base, proceeds, and income keep the strict precedence rule above.

### Overview

```
POST /portfolio/overview
```

Returns open holdings per **(listing, holding account)**, as at today (see [As-at date](#as-at-date)) — the same listing held in two accounts (e.g. an employer share plan and a personal broker account) reports as two holdings. Request body (optional):

```json
{ "live": true, "prices": { "<listing_id>": "<price>" } }
```

Response fields per holding: `listing_id`, `holding_account_id`, `quantity`, `avg_cost_base_per_unit`, `total_cost_base`, `current_price` (nullable), `market_value` (nullable), `price_as_of` (nullable), `price_unavailable` (nullable).

Cost base is calculated as `(price × quantity + brokerage + GST) − AMIT reductions`, pro-rated to remaining (unsold) units, less [return-of-capital](#corporate-actions) payments received on those units — flooring at nil (CGT events E10 and G1) — then converted to AUD (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)). Supplied prices are expected in AUD, so `market_value` is AUD. The unrealised-gains report computes its cost base the same way. `quantity` is in *current* units — re-based across any [share splits/consolidations or bonus issues](#corporate-actions) — so it lines up with a current market price; the re-basing never changes the cost base totals.

**Live valuation (`live`):** see [Live valuation](#live-valuation) — with `live: true` each held listing with no explicit price is valued from the [price source](#closing-prices)'s latest quote (converted to AUD), and `price_as_of` carries the provider's quote timestamp; an unavailable quote sets `price_unavailable` and leaves the holding unvalued. The web UI defaults to live; the API defaults to off (so existing callers are unchanged).

The three price-taking reports (overview, [unrealised gains](#unrealised-gains), [performance](#performance)) are also run automatically against the stored [closing prices](#closing-prices) each day and persisted as [report snapshots](#report-snapshots); the request-supplied `prices` remain for ad-hoc what-if runs.

### Live valuation

The three price-taking reports — [overview](#overview), [unrealised gains](#unrealised-gains), and [performance](#performance) — accept `"live": true` to value holdings from the **current** price at the [price source](#closing-prices) (Yahoo) instead of returning empty valuations when no `prices` are supplied. Each held listing without an explicit price is valued from the provider's latest available quote, in the listing's quote currency, **converted to AUD** at the quote-month ATO rate — currencies are never mixed.

- **As-of time:** every live-valued row carries `price_as_of`, the provider's quote timestamp (RFC 3339 UTC) — how fresh the valuation is. The UI rolls the per-row times up into one "as at …" line; an explicitly supplied price has no `price_as_of`.
- **Explicit override:** a price in `prices` always wins and is never fetched — what-if valuations and the deterministic acceptance tests keep working unchanged.
- **Provisional FX:** early in a month the quote month's ATO rate cannot exist yet (the RBA publishes after month end), so the conversion falls back to the most recent earlier month's rate (at most 2 months back) and the row is flagged `fx_provisional: true` — never silently substituted. The UI rolls the flags up into a "valued at a provisional FX rate" note on the "as at" line. This valuation-only fallback never reaches a tax calculation (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)).
- **Graceful failure:** a per-listing fetch failure (provider error, a currency mismatch, or an FX-rate gap older than the fallback bound) does not zero the holding or fail the request — that row is left unvalued (`current_price`/`market_value` null) with the reason in `price_unavailable`, while the rest of the report still values (consistent with the never-silent-zero rule).

`live` defaults to **off** so existing API callers and the deterministic ATO acceptance tests never hit the network; the web UI sets it on by default. This is on-demand live valuation only — it does not write to the [closing-price history](#closing-prices) or the daily [report snapshots](#report-snapshots), which remain sourced from stored closing prices.

### Open parcels

```
GET /portfolio/open-parcels
```

Returns every open parcel as at today (see [As-at date](#as-at-date)) — a Buy/DRP trade whose quantity is not fully consumed by parcel allocations — the per-parcel cost-base schedule to reconcile against a broker statement and the input to a sell decision (the [overview](#overview) aggregates the same parcels per listing). Response fields per parcel: `trade_id`, `listing_id`, `holding_account_id` (the account the parcel sits in), `ticker`, `acquisition_date`, `original_quantity`, `remaining_quantity` (units not yet allocated to a Sell), `original_cost_base` (price × quantity + brokerage + GST for the whole parcel), `amit_cost_base_reduction` (cumulative [AMIT reductions](#amit-adjustments) reaching the *remaining* units to date — the full amount, even where CGT event E10 has floored the cost base; a statement covering only the units still held is reported at what it took off them, not the whole parcel), `return_of_capital_reduction` (cumulative [return-of-capital](#corporate-actions) payments received on the remaining units since acquisition — likewise the full amount, even where CGT event G1 has floored the cost base), and `remaining_cost_base` (the remaining units' share of `original_cost_base`, less the AMIT and return-of-capital reductions reaching them, floored at nil). All monetary fields are AUD, converted at the parcel's buy-month rate (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)). `remaining_quantity` is in *current* units — re-based across any [share splits/consolidations or bonus issues](#corporate-actions) so it reconciles with a broker statement — while `original_quantity` stays as transacted; `acquisition_date` is preserved across a split or bonus issue (TD 2000/10; `docs/ato/bonus-shares.md`). A [scrip-for-scrip](#corporate-actions) replacement parcel reports the consumed parcel's acquisition date (the rollover's combined holding period) and carries its remaining reduced cost base; its monetary fields convert at the *original* acquisition month's rate, so the AUD cost base is unchanged by the exchange. A [demerger's](#corporate-actions) head and demerged parcels likewise report the consumed parcel's acquisition date, each carrying its percentage share of that cost base.

A [transfer's](#transfers) transfer-in parcel likewise reports the moved parcel's acquisition date and carries its share of the remaining reduced cost base, in the destination account.

Sorted by `listing_id`, then `holding_account_id`, then `acquisition_date`, then `trade_id`.

### Attachments index

```
GET /reports/attachments
```

The whole-portfolio [attachment](#attachments) register — every stored document, joined out to the activity it is attached to — where the per-owner attachments view only ever shows one activity's files at a time. Response fields per row: `id`, `filename`, `content_type`, `byte_size`, `uploaded_at` (the same metadata `GET /attachments` returns), `owner_type` (`Trade`, `Income`, `AMMA statement`, `ESS statement`, `Interest income`, or `Corporate action` — which of the six owner FKs is set), `owner_field` (the matching query key — `trade_id`, `income_id`, etc. — for `GET /attachments?<owner_field>=<owner_id>`), `owner_id`, `owner_description` (a human summary of the owning row, e.g. "Buy on 2024-05-01" or "FY ending 2024-06-30"), and `listing_id` (the owning activity's listing; null only for an interest-income attachment, which has no listing). The web UI's row actions link to `GET /attachments/:id/content` (Download), the same URL with `?disposition=inline` (View, opened in a new tab), and the owning activity's own attachments view (Record).

Sorted by `uploaded_at` descending, then `id` descending (newest upload first).

### Listing activity

```
POST /portfolio/activity
```

Everything ever recorded against **one listing**, in chronological order, ending in the final holding summary (units held, cost base, current value). Request body:

```json
{ "listing_id": 1, "price": "12.50" }
```

`listing_id` (required) must exist (`404` otherwise). `price` (optional) is the current per-unit price in AUD for the holding summary; absent, it is live-fetched per the [live-valuation rules](#live-valuation), degrading per holding (`price_unavailable`) when no quote is obtainable — the ledger itself never needs a price.

Response: `listing_id`, `events` (the ledger), and `holdings` (the final summary). Every input is read on one read transaction, so the ledger and the summary come from a single consistent snapshot.

**`events`** — one row per recorded fact, sorted by date (a corporate action orders before same-dated trades: a trade dated on a split's conversion date is already in post-split units). Row kinds:

- every **trade**, labelled with the operation that created it — a plain `Buy`/`Sell`, `DRP reinvestment`, or `Buy (rights exercise)`, `Sell (buy-back)`, `Buy/Sell (scrip exchange)`, `Buy/Sell (demerger)`, `Sell (worthless shares)`, `Buy (ESS vest)`, `Buy (inheritance)`, `Sell (transfer network fee)` — **except** a [transfer](#transfers) group's own Sell/Buys, which collapse into the transfer's one row (a transfer is not a disposal and nets to nothing)
- **transfers between accounts** (one row: moved units, source and destination accounts, any network-fee disposal noted)
- **income** (`Dividend`, `Trust distribution`, `Dividend (buy-back component)`, with franking credits and any reinvestment link in the detail)
- **corporate actions** (return of capital, share split/consolidation, bonus issue, rights issue, buy-back offer, scrip-for-scrip takeover, demerger, worthless shares — each with its per-unit amount / ratio / terms). A scrip-for-scrip or demerger row names its **counterpart listing** by the ticker that listing carried **as at the action's own date** (see [Ticker or name changes](#listings)), so a counterpart [renamed](#listings) since still reads the way it did when the action happened; a counterpart never renamed reads its current ticker
- **AMMA statements** (dated by their 30 June year end), **ESS statements** (dated by the taxing point), **rights sales/lapses**, **DRP enrolment/unenrolment**, and **listing-scoped [investment expenses](#investment-expenses)**
- **`Ticker/exchange change`** — a recorded [rename](#listings), dated at its `effective_date`, detailing the old and new ticker (and old/new exchange, when the rename moved it)

Fields per row: `date`, `event`, `detail` (human-readable specifics in the record's own currency), `holding_account_id` (nullable — corporate actions and transfers span accounts), `quantity` (nullable: the row's signed unit effect, in its own date's unit basis), `units_after` (the whole-listing running balance after the row — a split/bonus issue re-bases it in place, a transfer leaves it unchanged; the last row's balance equals the holding summary's total quantity), and `amount_aud` (nullable: the row's own money figure in AUD — a trade's whole consideration `quantity × price ± brokerage and GST` converted with the trade's own [FX precedence](#fx-conversion); an income row's gross cash converted by the month the [tax summary](#tax-summary) uses; a rights sale's proceeds; an expense's deductible amount — absent where the row has no single amount, e.g. a per-unit return of capital).

**`holdings`** — the [overview](#overview)'s rows for the listing, one per holding account (`quantity`, `avg_cost_base_per_unit`, `total_cost_base`, and the `current_price` / `market_value` / `price_as_of` / `price_unavailable` valuation fields). An explicit `price` wins over the live fetch, exactly as in the overview.

### Parcel-selection optimiser

```
POST /portfolio/parcel-optimiser
```

Candidate parcel selections for a **contemplated** sale — which parcels a sale comes from is the taxpayer's choice (`docs/ato/cgt-keeping-records-shares.md`: Boris nominates the parcel that realises a loss), and it changes the tax outcome. **Read-only**: nothing is persisted; the user picks a candidate's allocations and enters them on the real [Sell](#sells). Request body:

```json
{ "listing_id": 1, "holding_account_id": 1, "units": "1500",
  "sale_date": "2026-06-15", "price": "8.00" }
```

`units` (required, positive) may not exceed the listing's open quantity in the account **as at `sale_date`** (`422` otherwise — a Sell may only consume its own account's parcels). `sale_date` (optional, default today) drives the 12-month discount clock *and* dates the candidate read (see below). `price` is the per-unit sale price in AUD; absent, it is **live-fetched** per the [live-valuation rules](#live-valuation) — but unlike the valuation reports, which leave a row unvalued, no obtainable price rejects the request with `422` and the reason (the candidates can't be valued without one).

The candidate parcels are the [open-parcels](#open-parcels) rows **as at `sale_date`** (see [As-at date](#as-at-date)) — the parcels a real [Sell](#sells) dated then could allocate, with their adjusted AUD cost base — so every cost-base rule — AMIT/E10, return of capital/G1, splits, rollover carried dates and cost bases — flows through unchanged. A parcel acquired *after* `sale_date` is not a candidate (a Sell refuses an allocation of one), and a parcel sold *since* `sale_date` is, because it was open then; `units`, `price` and the reported quantities are all in `sale_date`'s unit basis, so a split between that date and today never mixes bases. The hypothetical sale carries no brokerage (it isn't known yet). Four strategies are returned, each allocating the units greedily in its preference order (ties broken FIFO):

- `fifo` — oldest acquisition first; the no-choice baseline
- `min_gain` — smallest **assessable** contribution per unit first: losses (in full), then discount-eligible gains at half weight, then non-discountable gains — minimises the current-year assessable gain
- `max_discount` — discount-eligible gain parcels first, then losses, then non-discountable gains last — maximises the proportion of the realised gain that gets the 50% discount
- `harvest_losses` — loss parcels first (largest per-unit loss first), then FIFO

Response: the echoed inputs (`listing_id`, `holding_account_id`, `units`, `sale_date`), the `price` used with `price_as_of` (the provider quote timestamp, only when live-fetched), `strategies` — one record per strategy with the [realised-gains](#realised-gains) buckets for its candidate disposal (`proceeds`, `cost_base`, `capital_gain_loss`, `discount_eligible_gain`, `non_discountable_gain`, `capital_loss`; the identity `capital_gain_loss == discount_eligible_gain + non_discountable_gain − capital_loss` holds per strategy) — and `allocations`, the per-parcel rows keyed back by `strategy`: `purchase_trade_id`, `holding_account_id`, `acquisition_date`, `units`, `cost_base`, `proceeds`, `capital_gain_loss`, `discount_eligible` (held strictly more than 12 months at the sale date). All amounts are AUD; proceeds are spread over a strategy's allocations by quantity as a cumulative difference, so they sum exactly to `price × units`. The response also carries `taxpayer_basis` (informational): the **Australian-resident-individual** assumption the hard-wired 50% CGT discount rests on — other entity types (SMSF/complying super 33⅓%, company 0%, trust/partnership flow-through) are not modelled (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)). It is stated once for the whole response rather than per strategy row because the strategies are *ranked against each other* on that discount (`min_gain` weights a discount-eligible gain at half, `max_discount` orders on eligibility): on a different basis the recommendation itself — not merely a figure on it — would differ.

### Unrealised gains

```
POST /portfolio/unrealised-gains
```

Request body (all optional):

```json
{ "live": true, "prices": { "<listing_id>": "<price>" }, "as_of_date": "YYYY-MM-DD" }
```

`as_of_date` defaults to today, and the report is the position **as at** that date: trades, sales, corporate actions, and AMIT adjustments (by their statement's year end) dated after it are excluded. One row per (listing, holding account), like the [overview](#overview). Response fields per holding: `listing_id`, `holding_account_id`, `quantity`, `total_cost_base`, `current_price`, `market_value`, `unrealised_gain_loss`, `cgt_discount_eligible_quantity` (units from parcels held strictly more than 12 months as at `as_of_date`), `price_as_of` (nullable), `price_unavailable` (nullable). With `live: true`, unpriced holdings are valued from the price source's latest quote — see [Live valuation](#live-valuation). `total_cost_base` is in AUD (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)); supplied prices are expected in AUD, so `market_value` and `unrealised_gain_loss` are AUD. Quantities are in the unit basis of `as_of_date` — re-based across any [share splits/consolidations or bonus issues](#corporate-actions) up to that date — and neither a split nor a bonus issue restarts the 12-month discount clock (the converted/bonus shares keep the original acquisition date; TD 2000/10, `docs/ato/bonus-shares.md`). A [scrip-for-scrip](#corporate-actions) replacement or [demerger](#corporate-actions) head/demerged parcel's discount clock likewise runs from its deemed (carried) acquisition date — the rollover's combined holding period.

### Realised gains

```
GET /portfolio/realised-gains
```

Returns one record per disposal: each sale trade that has at least one parcel allocation, plus each [rights sale](#selling-or-lapsing-rights). Response fields: `source` (`"Sell"` — an ordinary Sell trade — or `"RightsSale"`), `sale_trade_id` (the disposal's row id in its source's table: `trades` for `Sell`, `rights_sales` for `RightsSale`), `listing_id`, `holding_account_id` (the account the Sell happened in — one taxpayer either way, so totals are account-independent), `sale_date`, `proceeds`, `cost_base`, `capital_gain_loss`, `discount_eligible_gain` (gross gain from parcels held strictly more than 12 months), `non_discountable_gain` (gross gain from parcels held 12 months or less — the "other" method), and `capital_loss` (total losses from allocations sold below cost, as a positive amount). The three buckets satisfy `capital_gain_loss == discount_eligible_gain + non_discountable_gain − capital_loss`. Every record also carries `taxpayer_basis` (informational: the **Australian-resident-individual** 50%-discount assumption behind the `discount_eligible_gain` / `non_discountable_gain` split — other entity types are not modelled, see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)), stated per row as the [tax summary](#tax-summary) and [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) rows state theirs. `proceeds`, `cost_base`, and `capital_gain_loss` are in AUD: proceeds are converted at the sale's FX rate and cost base at the purchase's FX rate (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)). The cost base of the sold units is reduced by [return-of-capital](#corporate-actions) payments received while they were held — from acquisition up to the sale date — flooring at nil; payments after the sale don't touch them. An allocation's quantity is in the sale date's unit basis: a [share split/consolidation or bonus issue](#corporate-actions) between purchase and sale re-bases it back to as-acquired units for the cost-base pro-rating, and the discount holding period still runs from the original acquisition date (TD 2000/10; `docs/ato/bonus-shares.md`). A `RightsSale` row is the disposal of the rights themselves: proceeds `units × proceeds_per_right` and cost base `rights_cost` (nil for free rights), both converted at the sale month, with each anchoring allocation's discount clock running from its original parcel's (possibly deemed) acquisition date — sold rights inherit the original shares' acquisition date, unlike an exercised parcel whose clock restarts at exercise. A [scrip-for-scrip](#corporate-actions) exchange's or [demerger's](#corporate-actions) closing Sell is **excluded** — the rollover disregards its gain — as is a [transfer's](#transfers) transfer-out Sell (an own-account move is no disposal at all; a later sale of the transfer-in parcel uses the carried cost base and deemed acquisition date) — and a sale of a replacement (or demerger head/demerged) parcel uses the carried (apportioned) cost base, converted at the original acquisition month's rate, with the discount clock running from the deemed (carried) acquisition date, the rollover's combined holding period.

**Where a Sell's brokerage and GST land.** A Sell's `brokerage` and `gst_on_brokerage` are **netted off `proceeds`** — pro-rated across the sale's allocations — and are *not* added to `cost_base`. The ATO's own worked examples present the same disposal the other way round: full capital proceeds against a cost base that includes the incidental costs of the disposal (`docs/ato/cgt-cost-base.md`, second element — costs "that relate to the CGT event"). **The capital gain is identical either way**; only the split between the two reported components differs, by the disposal costs. A 100-unit sale at $12 with $10.945 of brokerage + GST, out of a parcel bought for $1,010.945, reports `proceeds: 1189.055` / `cost_base: 1010.945`, where an ATO worksheet shows $1,200.00 / $1,021.89 — both give a $178.11 gain. The netting convention is deliberate: it keeps `proceeds` the cash actually received, and keeps `cost_base` the *same* figure the [open parcels](#open-parcels) and [unrealised gains](#unrealised-gains) reports show for that parcel while it is still held, so a parcel's cost base doesn't move the moment it is sold. If you are reconciling a figure against an ATO worksheet, expect the gain to agree and the two components to differ by the sale's costs.

Each disposal also carries `parcels`: the individual parcel allocations behind its totals — `purchase_trade_id` (the anchor parcel for a `RightsSale`), `acquisition_date`, `units`, `cost_base`, `proceeds`, `capital_gain_loss`, and `discount_eligible` (held strictly more than 12 months at the sale date). These are the same per-allocation figures the disposal's own totals are summed from, sorted by acquisition date then `purchase_trade_id`, so a UI can drill from the disposal into which parcel contributed what without a separate request.

Sorted by `sale_date` ascending (then source, then id).

### Performance

```
POST /portfolio/performance
Body (optional): { "live": true, "prices": { "<listing_id>": "12.34", ... }, "as_of_date": "2026-06-30" }
```

Investment performance (not tax) per holding (listing × holding account) plus a final **OVERALL** row, valued at `as_of_date` (default: today; trades and income dated after it are ignored) with the supplied AUD prices. The report is cash-flow based: **out** — each Buy/DRP parcel's AUD cost on its trade date (converted at the acquisition month, or the deemed acquisition month for a rollover-created parcel); **in** — each Sell's AUD net proceeds, each distribution's cash (franked + unfranked + foreign source − foreign tax − TFN withholding; franking credits are not cash), and the holding's market value at `as_of_date`. Internal movements — [transfers](#transfers), [scrip-for-scrip exchanges, and demergers](#corporate-actions) — are valued **at the carried cost** within each holding (the source exits without gain; the destination carries the cost base, so the gain shows up where the parcels now sit) and are skipped entirely in the OVERALL row, which sees only external cash. AMMA statements attribute taxable income, not cash, and are excluded; a DRP reinvestment is both cash income and a same-sized purchase, so it nets out.

Response fields per row: `listing_id`, `ticker`, `holding_account_id`, `quantity_held` (as-of units; all three `null`/`"OVERALL"` on the total row), `invested`, `proceeds`, `income` (lifetime AUD figures), `market_value` (`quantity_held` × the supplied or live price), `total_return` (proceeds + income + market value − invested, AUD), `total_return_pct` (of invested), `money_weighted_return_pct` (annualised internal rate of return over the dated flows, actual/365, % p.a.), `income_yield_pct` (trailing 12 months' income / market value), `price_as_of` (nullable; absent on the OVERALL row), and `price_unavailable` (nullable). A still-open holding with no supplied price reports `null` for every market-dependent metric rather than a silently wrong figure; the OVERALL row likewise reports them only when every open holding is priced. With `live: true`, unpriced open holdings are valued from the price source's latest quote — see [Live valuation](#live-valuation).

### Period performance

```
POST /portfolio/period-performance
Body: { "from": "2026-04-25", "to": "2026-07-25" }
```

How the portfolio's AUD value changed between two dates, attributed to capital growth, FX movement, and cash income. The window is half-open `(from, to]` — a trade or income payment dated `from` is already inside the opening value, matching [Performance](#performance)'s cumulative-to-`as_of` convention. `from` must be strictly before `to`, or the request fails with `422`.

Both endpoints are valued from **stored** closing prices only (never a live fetch) via the same valuation path [Report snapshots](#report-snapshots) generation uses: each held listing's nearest trading day at or before the date, converted at the [valuation FX rate](#fx-conversion) (the ATO monthly rate, or a fallback earlier month's when not yet published, flagging the result `provisional`). A held listing with no final stored price at either endpoint fails the whole request with `422` (the same "no partial result" rule snapshot generation follows), except one marked [`unpriced_from`](#listings), which is valued at its carried-forward close and sets `price_carried_forward`, or one marked [`unpriced_before`](#listings) at a date before its provider series begins, which is left out of that endpoint's total and sets `holding_excluded` — pass dates already covered by a stored [snapshot](#report-snapshots) (e.g. from `GET /report_snapshots/series`) to avoid this.

Per holding `h` (listing × holding account), with `V0`/`V1` its opening/closing AUD market value (0 when not held at that end):

- **Income** = cash income (franked + unfranked + foreign source − foreign tax − TFN withholding — the same definition as the DRP-reinvestable amount) received in the window.
- **FX movement** = the *closing* native-currency exposure revalued at the opening vs closing FX rate: `closing_units × closing_native_price × (1/rate_to − 1/rate_from)`. Exactly zero for an AUD listing or a holding fully closed by `to` — the approximation this implies (a holding traded inside the window carries none of its own FX movement) is deliberate; see Known limitations below.
- **Capital growth** = the residual: `total_return − FX movement − income`.

These three always sum exactly to `total_return` by construction — `total_return` itself is [Performance](#performance)'s cumulative total return at `to` minus at `from` (that computation already handles [transfers, scrip-for-scrip exchanges, and demergers](#corporate-actions) correctly at portfolio level), not recomputed from scratch.

Response fields: `from`, `to`, `opening_market_value`, `closing_market_value`, `purchases` (AUD cost of Buy/DRP trades in the window), `sale_proceeds` (AUD net Sell proceeds in the window), `income`, `capital_growth`, `fx_movement`, `total_return`, `total_return_pct` (`total_return / opening_market_value × 100`; `null` when the window opened with nothing held — **not** cash-flow aware: a window with large purchases partway through divides by the small opening balance alone, so it reads far bigger than the money actually earned), `money_weighted_return_pct` (the window's annualised money-weighted return — an internal rate of return, actual/365, same method as [Performance](#performance)'s own `money_weighted_return_pct` — over the portfolio's external cash flows in the window plus the opening market value as an outflow at `from` and the closing market value as an inflow at `to`; correct regardless of how much of the closing value came from money added during the window rather than growth of the opening balance; `null` when all flows fall on one day or the flows admit no rate), `realised_capital_gain` (the **tax** realised capital gain — [realised-gains report](#realised-gains) rows with `sale_date` in the window, summed — informational only, reported alongside the breakdown but **not** part of the additive capital/FX/income split; see Known limitations), `provisional` (any conversion at either endpoint used a fallback-month FX rate, same convention as snapshots), `price_carried_forward` (a holding at either endpoint was valued at a **carried-forward** close because its listing is marked [`unpriced_from`](#listings) — its capital growth over the window is measured against a stale native price, so a window straddling the date the quotes stopped shows the fall to the last close and nothing after it; kept separate from `provisional` for the same reason [snapshots](#report-snapshots) keep them apart), `holding_excluded` with `excluded_holdings` (a holding at either endpoint had **no obtainable price** there because its listing is marked [`unpriced_before`](#listings) and was left out of that endpoint's total — a window straddling the day the provider's series begins therefore books the holding's whole closing value as capital growth; the list names each absent holding and why), `holdings` (the same per-holding fields, keyed `listing_id`/`holding_account_id`), and `fx_by_currency` (per currency: `fx_movement`, and `rate_from`/`rate_to` — omitted, not zeroed, when the currency's listings resolved different rate pairs at an endpoint — plus `provisional`).

The web UI's Portfolio Overview performance panel shows `total_return_pct` (as "Return %") for a selected date range up to a year, and switches to `money_weighted_return_pct` (as "Return % (p.a.)") beyond a year — the point at which a raw, non-annualised percentage stops being legible and cash-flow timing starts to matter.

`holdings` includes a row for **every** holding with any history up to either endpoint, including one fully closed well before `from` — every field on such a row is exactly zero. The endpoint returns these rows unfiltered; the web UI's per-holding contributions table hides them by default (a "hide holdings with no activity in this period" checkbox, checked unless the user last unchecked it) so real movers aren't buried in zero-rows — a holding only counts as no-activity when `opening_market_value`, `closing_market_value`, `purchases`, `sale_proceeds`, and `income` are all exactly zero (which forces `capital_growth`/`fx_movement`/`total_return` to zero too); a holding that was merely flat over the period still shows.

### Net capital gain

```
GET /portfolio/net-capital-gain
```

Returns one record per Australian financial year (identified by the calendar year of 30 June), sorted ascending — the overall CGT position combining realised parcel gains with the CGT components attributed on AMMA statements. Realised gains are attributed by the sale's tax year (July = next FY); AMMA components by `tax_year_end_date`. A [scrip-for-scrip](#corporate-actions) exchange or [demerger](#corporate-actions) contributes nothing in the exchange/demerger year — the rollover disregards the gain; the deferred gain surfaces when the replacement (or head/demerged) parcels are eventually sold.

**Which years get a record.** Every year with recorded CGT activity, **plus every quiet year that still carries a capital loss forward** — a year with no gain, loss or CGT event of its own but a non-zero brought-forward balance is reported with zero gains, zero current-year losses, and that balance on both `capital_loss_brought_forward` and `capital_loss_carried_forward`. Label **18V** (*net capital losses carried forward to later income years*) is reported every year until the loss is used, not only in years with a CGT event (`docs/ato/capital-gains-question-18.md`, step 11), so the year in which the investor simply held everything still has an 18V figure to report. These filler rows run through to the financial year **in progress** — the last year a return could be being prepared for, and nothing beyond it exists to report — which is also why a database whose only content is the [`cgt_settings`](#cgt-settings) opening loss (a pre-system balance attributed to no year) reports that loss against the current year. A year with neither activity nor a balance gets no record at all: the series stays sparse, an activity list plus the years that actually owe an 18V figure — it is **not** a continuous year-by-year series.

The assessable net capital gain is computed the ATO way:

1. Total the year's gross capital gains, split into **discount-eligible** (realised parcels held > 12 months + AMMA discount-method gains grossed up ×2 — the AMMA `cgt_discount_gains` value is the already-halved "discounted capital gain", so doubling it restores the gross gain + any **CGT event E10/G1/C2** gain whose parcel was held > 12 months at the event date) and **non-discountable** (realised parcels held ≤ 12 months + AMMA indexation-method and other-method gains, neither of which gets the discount + any CGT event E10/G1/C2 gain held ≤ 12 months).
2. Total the year's capital losses: realised losses, **plus the net capital loss brought forward from earlier years** — unused losses chain across the year series indefinitely (per the ATO), starting from the entered [opening carried-forward loss](#cgt-settings) (losses from before the first recorded year). An AMMA statement's `capital_losses_applied` is deliberately **not** counted: those losses were applied at the *trust* level before attribution — the statement's CGT amounts are already net of them, and a trust cannot distribute capital losses to members, so only the investor's own losses enter the netting (`docs/ato/amma-statement-guidance-notes.md`; `docs/ato/personal-investors-guide-managed-fund-distributions.md`, Step 4).
3. Apply losses against non-discountable gains first, then discount-eligible gains (taxpayer-favourable: the 50% discount falls on the largest possible remaining gain). Losses always apply before the discount.
4. **Net capital gain** = remaining non-discountable gain + 50% of the remaining discount-eligible gain. Unused losses are carried forward into the next year in the series.

The 50% rate is the **Australian-resident-individual** CGT discount — other taxpayer entity types (SMSF/complying super 33⅓%, company 0%, trust/partnership flow-through) are not modelled (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)). Every row states this in its informational `taxpayer_basis` field rather than leaving the assumption implicit.

**CGT event E10**: when the cumulative AMIT cost base reductions on a parcel (each adjustment's covered quantity, re-based into its statement year's unit basis, × that AMMA statement's per-unit `cost_base_adjustment` — see [AMIT adjustments](#amit-adjustments)) exceed its cost base, the cost base is floored at nil (in the portfolio, unrealised, and realised reports) and the excess is a capital gain in the income year the reducing AMMA statement applies to — added to the gain buckets above (discount-eligible vs not, per the holding period as at the statement's `tax_year_end_date`). The excess is converted to AUD at the parcel's buy-month rate. See `docs/ato/amit-cost-base-adjustments.md`.

**CGT event G1**: when a company's cumulative [return-of-capital](#corporate-actions) payments exceed a parcel's per-unit cost base, the cost base is floored at nil and the excess is a capital gain in the payment's income year — covering only the units still held at the payment date, and never producing a capital loss. The gain is added to the gain buckets above (discount-eligible vs not, per the holding period as at the payment date) and converted to AUD at the payment month's ATO rate (no manual fallback: a non-AUD payment with no rate fails loudly with `422`, naming the currency and month). See `docs/ato/cgt-non-assessable-payments.md`.

**CGT event C2**: the other side of the same payment — units the parcel held at the action's `record_date` but **sold before the payment date**. They receive the payment without owning the shares when it is made, so no cost base is reduced (G1 cannot apply) and the sale's own realised figures are untouched; instead the *right to receive* the payment ends on the payment date, CGT event C2. Its cost base is nil wherever the share's own cost base was fully applied in working out the gain or loss on the disposal — which an ordinary Sell always does — so the capital gain is the **whole payment** on those units, in the payment's income year. It joins the gain buckets above with the discount measured on the **share's** holding period to the payment date (the same test G1 uses, not the right's own record-date-to-payment life) and converts at the payment month's ATO rate like G1. Reported informationally as `cgt_event_c2_gain`. This needs the action's `record_date`: with none recorded, entitlement falls back to the payment date and units sold earlier are simply not entitled, so nothing is reported. See `docs/ato/return-of-capital-right-to-receive.md` (Class Ruling CR 2025/59).

**One chain per parcel**: the two events are *not* walked separately. A parcel's AMIT decreases and return-of-capital payments draw down **one running cost base**, in the date order they arise (an AMMA statement at its `tax_year_end_date`, a payment at its payment date — AMIT first where the two coincide), mirroring the single balance the [cost-base pipeline](#open-parcels) nets them against. Each excess is then attributed to the event that drove the balance past nil, keeping that event's own year, AUD rate and holding-period test as described above. Two independent walks would each report `its own reductions − cost base` where the truth is `all reductions − cost base`, understating the year's gain whenever a parcel carries both kinds — and reporting nothing at all when neither kind exceeds the cost base on its own. The combination is not hypothetical: a non-AMIT trust's CGT event E4 tax-deferred reduction is entered as a `ReturnOfCapital` action (see [Income](#income)), so a fund that converts to an AMIT part-way through a holding carries both against the same parcel — the payments dated in its pre-[`amit_from`](#listings) years, since a `ReturnOfCapital` dated in one of the listing's AMIT years is refused at write time (see [Corporate actions](#corporate-actions)). It is also entry-order independent — the chain sorts by event date, not by when the rows were recorded.

Response fields: `tax_year`, `discount_eligible_gains`, `other_gains`, `capital_losses` (all gross; `capital_losses` is only the losses arising that year), `capital_loss_brought_forward` (unused losses chained from earlier years, seeded by the `cgt_settings` opening balance), `net_discount_eligible_gain` and `net_other_gain` (after losses), `cgt_discount` (the 50% reduction applied = `net_discount_eligible_gain / 2`), `net_capital_gain`, `capital_loss_carried_forward` (losses left unused after offsetting all gains — the next year's brought-forward balance), `cgt_event_e10_gain`, `cgt_event_g1_gain`, `cgt_event_c2_gain` (informational: gross E10/G1/C2 gains already included in the gain buckets), `taxpayer_basis` (informational: the individual-resident rate assumption above), and `disposals` — the year's realised disposals (per the [realised-gains report](#realised-gains), each with its own nested `parcels`) that fed the discount-eligible/non-discountable/loss buckets above, so a UI can drill from a year down through its disposals to the individual parcels. AMMA-attributed and CGT event E10/G1/C2 gains have no parcel-allocation record and so are folded into the year's totals only, not into `disposals`. All amounts are AUD (AMMA amounts converted via the ATO rate for the month of `tax_year_end_date`, so a non-AUD amount with no rate fails loudly with `422`, naming the currency and month; see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)).

```
GET /portfolio/net-capital-gain/export
```

The same per-year records as a downloadable tax-return-ready CSV (`Content-Type: text/csv; charset=utf-8`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="net-capital-gain.csv"`): a header row naming the columns (the response fields above, **excluding `disposals`** — the csv writer rejects a record with a nested sequence, and the export was already the flat per-year record before the JSON drilldown was added), a **second header row carrying each column's ATO tax-return label**, then one record per financial year the report covers (a quiet year carrying a loss forward exports like any other — see Which years get a record above). An empty report still returns both header rows.

The label row's first cell is `ato_labels_2026`, naming the form year the mapping targets — the **Individual tax return 2026** (paper supplementary section; myTax shows the same labels). Labels shift year to year; the verified reference is `docs/ato/tax-return-labels-2026.md`. The mapping (columns not listed report at no label — intermediate workings or informational figures):

| Column | Label | Meaning |
|--------|-------|---------|
| `discount_eligible_gains` + `other_gains` | `18H (component)` | The two gross-gain columns **sum** to label 18H, *Total current year capital gains* (AMMA discount gains already grossed up ×2 in `discount_eligible_gains`) |
| `capital_losses`, `net_discount_eligible_gain`, `net_other_gain`, `cgt_discount` | `18 (working)` | Steps of question 18's calculation with no label of their own |
| `capital_loss_brought_forward` | `18V (prior year)` | Equals label 18V from the **previous** year's return |
| `net_capital_gain` | `18A` | *Net capital gain* |
| `capital_loss_carried_forward` | `18V` | *Net capital losses carried forward to later income years* |

### Pre-sale what-if

```
POST /portfolio/net-capital-gain/what-if
```

Dry-runs a **hypothetical** disposal through the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) computation and returns the disposal year's figures with and without it — **no rows are written**. The whole-of-income tax estimate is out of scope (consistent with the FITO decision); this is the CGT-side delta only. Request body:

```json
{ "listing_id": 1, "holding_account_id": 1, "units": "1500", "proceeds": "12000",
  "date": "2026-06-15",
  "allocations": [ { "purchase_trade_id": 2, "units": "1500" } ] }
```

`units` must be positive and `proceeds` (the total capital proceeds, **AUD**) non-negative. `date` (required) is the disposal's date: it fixes the tax year, the 12-month discount clock, *and* the candidate read. The sold units are drawn from the listing's [open parcels](#open-parcels) **as at `date`** (see [As-at date](#as-at-date); restricted to `holding_account_id`'s parcels when supplied) — the parcels a real [Sell](#sells) dated then could allocate, so a parcel acquired after `date` is not open for it and one sold since `date` is, and `units`, the implied per-unit price `proceeds ÷ units` and the reported quantities are all in `date`'s unit basis — via **exactly one** of:

- `allocations` — explicit per-parcel choices; each must name a parcel of the listing open **as at `date`** with enough remaining units then, and they must sum to `units` (`422` otherwise, as on a real [Sell](#sells) — including a parcel acquired after `date`, which a Sell rejects as "an allocated parcel is dated after the sale date")
- `strategy` — one of the [parcel-selection optimiser](#parcel-selection-optimiser)'s strategy names (`fifo`, `min_gain`, `max_discount`, `harvest_losses`); the allocations are derived the optimiser's way at the implied per-unit price `proceeds ÷ units`, rejecting `units` beyond the quantity open as at `date` with `422`

The hypothetical's gain/loss buckets (classified per parcel exactly as the [realised-gains](#realised-gains) report would: discount when held strictly more than 12 months at `date`, the cost base from the adjusted open-parcel figures) are injected into the disposal's tax year and the full loss-chaining walk re-run, so earlier years' carried-forward losses (and the [opening balance](#cgt-settings)) apply in both scenarios.

Response fields: `tax_year` (the disposal's financial year), `strategy` (echoed when one was named), `hypothetical` (the disposal's own buckets: `proceeds`, `cost_base`, `capital_gain_loss`, `discount_eligible_gain`, `non_discountable_gain`, `capital_loss`), `allocations` (its per-parcel rows, derived or as supplied — the optimiser's allocation fields), and `years` — exactly two [net-capital-gain year records](#net-capital-gain) for the disposal year, each tagged `scenario` = `"without"` / `"with"`; a year with no recorded activity still yields both rows (zeros plus the correct brought-forward chain). Later years' changed carry-forward is **not** returned — re-run the main report with the disposal entered for the full series.

### Tax summary

```
GET /portfolio/tax-summary
```

Returns one record per Australian financial year (identified by the calendar year of 30 June), sorted ascending. Aggregates dividend income by `date_paid` (July = next FY) — except a trust distribution carrying an `entitlement_date`, which is attributed by that date instead (trust income is assessed in the year of present entitlement regardless of payment: ATO QC 23087, `docs/ato/trust-income-timing.md`; see [Income](#income)) — and AMMA statements by `tax_year_end_date`. Income rows on an **AMIT listing are excluded entirely** for the years the listing was an AMIT (every component — cash, withholding, offsets; a converted fund's pre-[`amit_from`](#listings) years are ordinary trust income and are reported in full): they are [cash-only rows](#income) that fund the DRP chain, and for an AMIT the AMMA attribution is the only assessable record, reported on the `amma_*` lines — counting the cash too would double the year's income (the [AMIT cash cross-check](#amit-cash-cross-check) flags a cash year with no covering AMMA statement). All amounts are converted to AUD via the ATO rate (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)) before aggregating, using each record's `currency` and the month of the attribution date — `date_paid` (income; the governing `entitlement_date` for a trust row that has one) or `tax_year_end_date` (AMMA). Response fields include all income and AMMA components as separate fields for direct transfer to a tax return, plus the informational `taxpayer_basis` field stating the **Australian-resident-individual** assumption behind the hard-wired rates (the LIC capital gain deduction passed through here is the individual 50% figure; other entity types are not modelled — see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)).

**Franking-credit entitlement** (the at-risk holding-period rule, `docs/ato/you-and-your-shares-dividends.md`): `franking_credits` reports only *claimable* credits. In a year whose total attached credits (income + AMMA) reach A$5,000, each dividend's shares must have been held at risk for at least 45 days — 90 for a listing flagged `preference` — not counting the acquisition or disposal day; which shares were sold is identified **last-in first-out** (as the ATO mandates for this rule), regardless of the CGT parcel allocation chosen on the sale. Credits on entitled units that fail the test are reported in `franking_credits_denied` and excluded from `franking_credits` (the [franking at-risk report](#franking-at-risk) explains each denial — the failing window and units — and its what-if mode tests a contemplated sale before it is recorded). Below A$5,000 the small-shareholder exemption applies and nothing is denied. The test anchors on the income record's `ex_date` (falling back to `date_paid` when absent); AMMA-attributed credits count toward the threshold but are never themselves denied (an annual AMMA statement carries no per-distribution ex-date). A [demerger's](#corporate-actions) closing Sell and head replacement Buys are excluded from the walk — the head shares were never actually disposed of, so their at-risk days keep running across the demerger. The two remaining qualified-person conditions — the **30%-at-risk test** (a hedge, option or futures position means the days it covers can't be counted) and the **related payments rule** (which the small-shareholder exemption does not excuse) — are **not modelled**, and neither a hedge nor a related payment is recordable, so `franking_credits` assumes the holdings are unhedged and under no related-payment obligation (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)).

**Foreign income tax offset (FITO) cap** (`docs/ato/fito-limit.md`): `foreign_tax_offsets` (income `foreign_tax_paid` + AMMA `foreign_tax_credits` + the **apportioned** AMMA `foreign_tax_credits_capital_gains`, in AUD) reports the offset claimable without the ATO's offset-limit calculation — up to the A$1,000 de-minimis per year.

**Foreign tax on a foreign capital gain is apportioned first** (`docs/ato/fito-capital-gains-apportionment.md`, QC 104349 *When a FITO applies*; ATO ID 2010/175): "if only part of a foreign capital gain is assessable in Australia (for example, the gain is subject to the discount capital gains concessions in Division 115) the foreign tax paid on the gain must be apportioned accordingly", and for an AMIT distribution the Part C figure "must be reduced for discounted capital gains". The trustee reports it **grossed up** deliberately — the AMMA guidance notes state FITO is *not* reduced for a discount applied at trust level — so the reduction is the member's, and this report does it: `claimable = foreign_tax_credits_capital_gains × (discount + indexation + other gains) ÷ (2 × discount + indexation + other gains)`, the discount component being reported net and grossed up to the amount the tax was actually paid on. With only discount-method gains this halves it. `foreign_tax_offsets_cgt_discount_reduction` surfaces how much was apportioned away, so the statement's Part C figure and this line can be reconciled. **This applies only to what is recorded in `foreign_tax_credits_capital_gains`**, which defaults to 0 (migration `0032`): a statement whose Part C reports one combined foreign-tax figure keeps it all in `foreign_tax_credits` — claimable in full — until the capital-gains portion is moved across by hand. Nothing can infer the split from a combined total. Not modelled: the loss-*ordering* choice (offsetting capital losses first against domestic gains to maximise the foreign-taxed component of the net gain) and the nil-net-gain case, where the offset falls away entirely — a year with both foreign-taxed gains and capital losses needs checking by hand. A year's foreign tax above A$1,000 is reported in `foreign_tax_offset_excess` and excluded from `foreign_tax_offsets`: the limit calculation needs the taxpayer's full income-tax position (employment income, deductions, Medicare levy), which is outside this system's data, so the excess is claimable only to the extent the taxpayer's own offset-limit calculation supports it. **The trust path is the only capital-gains route to this line**: foreign tax on a disposal *you* make carries no field anywhere — a [Sell](#sells) has no foreign-tax column — and is worked out and claimed outside this tool (see [Known limitations](#known-limitations)).

**Employee share scheme discount** (`docs/ato/employee-share-schemes.md`): [ESS statements](#ess-statements) are aggregated by `taxing_point_date` (July = next FY) into `ess_discount_assessable` — the Item 12 assessable discount (labels D + E + F + G) **net of** the applied $1,000 taxed-upfront reduction — reported separately from dividend/trust income and in AUD (foreign-currency statements converted via the ATO rate for the taxing-point month; no rate ⇒ fails loudly with `422`, naming the currency and month). A label carrying a [statement-AUD override](#ess-statements) (the employer statement's stated AUD figure, converted at the release-date spot rate — what the ATO prefill carries) is reported **verbatim** instead of converted, so the summary matches the lodged return; labels without one keep the RBA conversion. `ess_taxed_upfront_reduction` surfaces the reduction applied (`min(A$1,000, the year's taxed-upfront-eligible discount)`). Like the FITO cap, the tool applies the de-minimis, but the **≤A$180,000 adjusted-taxable-income eligibility test is over income this system does not hold** — so it is **recorded per year** rather than computed: a year set `ess_taxed_upfront_reduction_eligible: false` in [tax year settings](#tax-year-settings) reports its taxed-upfront discount **unreduced**, with `ess_taxed_upfront_reduction` nil. Recording nothing (the default) applies the reduction, which is what every existing database does. The [annual tax report](#annual-tax-report) footnotes the condition under its ESS table whenever a reduction was applied, so the archived document states the assumption it rests on. `ess_foreign_source_discount` is the foreign-source portion (label A), a memo already within `ess_discount_assessable`. The ESS TFN amounts withheld join the existing `tfn_withholding_tax` line.

**Interest income** (`docs/ato/tax-return-labels-2026.md`): [interest income](#interest-income) records are aggregated by `date_paid` — the date credited (`docs/ato/investment-income-timing.md`; July = next FY) — each year's gross in AUD (foreign-currency amounts converted via the ATO rate for the month paid; no rate ⇒ fails loudly with `422`, naming the currency and month). An **Australian-source** row lands in `interest_income` — question 10's gross interest (10L), including any TFN amount withheld, the withheld amount itself joining the existing `tfn_withholding_tax` line. A row marked **`foreign_source`** (a foreign broker's cash / money-market fund) lands in `foreign_interest_income` instead — assessable foreign source income (20E) — with its `foreign_tax_paid` joining the `foreign_tax_offsets` FITO line (counting toward the A$1,000 de-minimis like any other foreign tax). Write-time validation keeps each withholding kind on the matching source, so the routing can't claim an offset a row doesn't support.

**Investment-expense deductions** (`docs/ato/investment-income-deductions.md`, `docs/ato/dividend-income-deductions.md`): [investment expenses](#investment-expenses) are aggregated by `date_incurred` (July = next FY) into per-type lines — `deductions_loan_interest`, `deductions_management_fee`, `deductions_advice_fee`, `deductions_account_keeping_fee`, `deductions_subscription`, `deductions_other` — and `deductions_total`, each the recorded post-apportionment deductible amount in AUD (foreign-currency expenses converted via the ATO rate for the month incurred; no rate ⇒ fails loudly with `422`, naming the currency and month).

**The same deductions are also cut by the question each is claimed at** (`docs/ato/tax-return-labels-2026.md`, *Where an investment-expense deduction goes*): the deductible amount is one figure, but the destination on the return follows the income the expense was incurred earning, and myTax cross-checks each question's deductions against the income that question carries. Four further lines re-cut the same total by destination:

| Line | Label | What lands there |
|------|-------|------------------|
| `deductions_trust_distributions` | `13Y` | Expenses of earning a **trust or AMIT** distribution — question 13's *other deductions relating to distributions* (non-primary production). Interest on money borrowed to buy the units is here too, not at D7: question 13 takes debt deductions ("debt deductions such as interest and borrowing costs, in deriving assessable income from a trust or partnership") |
| `deductions_foreign_income` | `20M` | Expenses of earning **foreign-source** income. Question 20's *other net foreign source income* is the gross less the expenses of earning it (worksheet 1, rows r − s), so this reduces 20M rather than being claimed at a deduction label of its own; 20E stays gross |
| `deductions_foreign_debt` | `D15` | A **debt** deduction (interest, borrowing costs) against foreign income — question 20's worksheet expressly excludes debt deductions, and D15 (label J) names foreign-source income as its example |
| `deductions_dividend_and_interest` | `D7 / D8` | Everything else: the expenses of earning Australian interest (D7) and Australian dividend/distribution income (D8) |

The destination is derived from the **listing the expense is attributed to** — a listing flagged `amit` (in any year: an AMIT and the ordinary trust it was before its `amit_from` year both report at question 13) or carrying any `trust_income` income row is a trust; otherwise a listing with any `foreign_source_income` income row earns foreign income, as does a non-AUD listing with no income recorded at all; and only `expense_type: LoanInterest` is treated as a debt deduction. Two cases are **not decidable** from what is recorded and take the D7/D8 default, which is where an ordinary share-investment expense belongs: a **portfolio-wide expense** (`listing_id: null`, including one attributed only to a holding account), which may relate to holdings across all three destinations with nothing recording how to apportion it; and an **AUD listing with no income recorded**, which nothing characterises. Split such an expense into one row per holding to route it. A holding whose distribution carries **both** trust and foreign components (a diversified Australian ETF, the ordinary case) routes wholly to 13Y — one expense row carries no component split — so split it across two rows to apportion it. Primary production (13X) is not modelled: a trust deduction always routes to 13Y. **The two cuts are of one total**: `deductions_total` is the sum of the six per-type lines *or* of the four destination lines, never of both, and the per-type lines carry no ATO label of their own (a management fee can be claimed at any of the four destinations). `gross_assessable_investment_income` sums the report's existing assessable income lines (`dividends_assessable` + `interest_income` + `foreign_interest_income` + `foreign_source_income` + the six AMMA income components), and `net_assessable_investment_income` = `gross_assessable_investment_income − deductions_total`. The gross figures are retained unchanged. The gross deliberately excludes the franking-credit gross-up and FITO (offset lines), the recorded conduit foreign income memo (already counted inside `dividends_assessable`, as part of `unfranked_amount` — see [Income](#income)), the ESS discount (employment income), and capital gains (the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) report); the LIC capital gain deduction is distinct and is not folded into the net figure.

```
GET /portfolio/tax-summary/export
```

The same per-year records as a downloadable tax-return-ready CSV (`Content-Type: text/csv; charset=utf-8`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="tax-summary.csv"`): a header row naming the columns (the response fields, in field order, from `tax_year` through `taxpayer_basis`), a **second header row carrying each column's ATO tax-return label**, then one record per financial year. An empty report still returns both header rows.

The label row's first cell is `ato_labels_2026`, naming the form year the mapping targets — the **Individual tax return 2026** (paper return + supplementary section; myTax shows the same labels). Labels shift year to year; the verified reference is `docs/ato/tax-return-labels-2026.md`. The mapping:

| Column | Label | Meaning |
|--------|-------|---------|
| `dividends_assessable` | `11S + 11T` | The single column is unfranked (11S) + franked (11T) dividends summed; split per the underlying income records |
| `interest_income` | `10L` | Australian gross interest, including any TFN amount withheld (the withheld amount is inside the `tfn_withholding_tax` column) |
| `foreign_interest_income`, `foreign_source_income`, `amma_foreign_income` | `20E + 20M` | Assessable foreign source income (20E gross; 20M is its net-of-expenses counterpart — with no foreign-side expenses recorded the two are equal); foreign-source interest reports here, never at 10L |
| `lic_capital_gain_deduction` | `D8` | Claimed at question D8 *Dividend deductions*: **50% of** each dividend's advised `lic_capital_gain_amount`, computed here — see [Income](#income) |
| `amma_australian_interest`, `amma_dividends_unfranked`, `amma_net_rent`, `amma_other_income` | `13U` | Non-primary production trust income components |
| `amma_franked_dividends` | `13C` | *Franked distributions from trusts* — on the return 13C **includes** the attached franking credits; this column is the statement's franked-distribution component |
| `amma_cgt_*` | `18 (working)` | Inputs to question 18 — the [net-capital-gain export](#net-capital-gain) carries the final 18H/18A/18V figures |
| `franking_credits` | `11U / 13Q` | Claimable credits from direct dividends (11U) and trust distributions (13Q), summed |
| `foreign_tax_offsets` | `20O` | FITO within the A$1,000 de-minimis (the excess column is unlabelled — claimable only per the taxpayer's own offset-limit calculation) |
| `tfn_withholding_tax` | `10M / 11V / 13R / 12C` | TFN credits from interest, dividends, trust distributions, and ESS discounts, summed |
| `ess_discount_assessable` | `12B` | *Total assessable discount amount*, already net of the applied $1,000 taxed-upfront reduction |
| `ess_foreign_source_discount` | `12A` | Foreign-source ESS discount memo (for the question 20 FITO claim) |
| `other_income` | `24` | *Other income* — an [`income_type: OtherIncome`](#income) row: a crypto staking reward or an established-token airdrop, at the tokens' receipt-date market value. Nothing prefills item 24, so unlike `employment_income` this line **is** inside `gross_assessable_investment_income` |
| `deductions_trust_distributions` | `13Y` | Expenses of earning a trust/AMIT distribution, including interest on money borrowed to buy the units |
| `deductions_foreign_income` | `20M` | Expenses of earning foreign-source income — subtracted on the way to 20M (20E stays gross), not a deduction label of its own |
| `deductions_foreign_debt` | `D15` | A debt deduction (interest/borrowing costs) against foreign income, which question 20's worksheet excludes |
| `deductions_dividend_and_interest` | `D7 / D8` | Expenses of earning Australian interest income at D7, dividend/distribution income at D8 — the split between the two questions is the taxpayer's, per where the income belongs |

Unlabelled columns are informational or derived: the six per-type `deductions_*` lines (`deductions_loan_interest` … `deductions_other` — the *kind* of expense, which is a different cut from the question it is claimed at, and so carries no label of its own) and `deductions_total` (the shared total of both cuts, spanning questions), `franking_credits_denied` and `foreign_tax_offset_excess` (amounts *excluded* from the claimable lines), `ess_taxed_upfront_reduction` (already inside 12B), `employment_income` (an [`income_type: EmploymentIncome`](#income) row — a dividend equivalent, which is remuneration at item 1/2 and normally already prefilled from the employer's STP reporting, so it is reported here to reconcile the cash and is in **no** assessable total of this report), `amma_capital_losses_applied` (trust-level losses the AMMA's gains are already net of — not a loss of the taxpayer's, so it feeds no question-18 figure), `gross_assessable_investment_income` / `net_assessable_investment_income` (derived totals), `taxpayer_basis`.

### Annual tax report

```
GET  /reports/tax-report/years
POST /reports/tax-report
```

A printable, per-year tax document meant to be saved to PDF and archived — distinct from the [tax summary](#tax-summary) above (which stays as the multi-year, spreadsheet-friendly all-years view). `GET /reports/tax-report/years` returns, ascending, every Australian financial year this report has content for — the union of everything it can report on, since the web UI's year picker is a closed `<select>` and the only way to reach the report: trades, interest, AMMA/ESS statements and investment expenses by their own dates; **income by its assessment date** (a trust distribution with a 30 June entitlement date paid in July is listed under the financial year just ended, the year it is assessed in — not the payment year); and every year the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) report's own year walk emits, which adds the CGT events that are not trades — realised disposals, rights sales, CGT events E10/G1/C2 — plus every quiet year still carrying a capital loss forward. A year with nothing in it and no loss balance is still absent, no year outside the accepted **1900–2999** `tax_year` range is ever offered, so every listed year is one `POST /reports/tax-report` answers, and no year **beyond the financial year in progress** is offered either — that is the last year a return could be being prepared for, and a later one has not begun. (A [trade](#trades) can no longer be dated in the future at all, but the list unions every dated fact, so the picker keeps its own ceiling rather than relying on one write path's.) `POST /reports/tax-report` takes `{ "tax_year": <int> }` — the calendar year the financial year **ends** in, accepted between **1900 and 2999** — and returns the whole document for that year; a year with no recorded activity returns a **zeroed document, not an error** — a mid-year draft is a legitimate use, as is the year after the one in progress. A `tax_year` outside that range is refused `422` naming the range: the bounds exclude input that is not a financial year at all (`0`, a negative year, a value no date can be built from — which formerly aborted the request), and sit far outside anything reportable rather than at a tax rule, so no year you could legitimately ask for is refused (the earliest year CGT can reach is FY1986, and only *trade* dates are floored there).

This report computes nothing new: every figure is sourced from the existing pipelines — [realised gains](#realised-gains) (the disposal schedule), [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) (the gain/loss summary), and the [tax summary](#tax-summary) above (the overall summary and every income figure) — plus the itemised cost-base adjustment detail those reports only expose netted. The response:

- **`meta`** — `tax_year`, `period_start`/`period_end` (1 July – 30 June), `generated_at` (the timestamp the document was produced, UTC), `taxpayer_basis`.
- **`completeness`** — non-blocking (never rejects the request): `amma_missing` lists every AMIT [listing](#listings) held at any point during the year (a non-zero opening balance, or any Buy/DRP dated within it) with no [AMMA statement](#amma-statements) covering it — **holdings-based**, so unlike the [AMIT cash cross-check](#amit-cash-cross-check) (which only fires when cash income rows exist) this also catches a fund-year where no cash rows were entered at all. It is asked **per holding account** (each row carries its `holding_account_id`), for the same reason that cross-check is: a registry issues one statement per holder account, so a fund held in two accounts needs two. Years before a converted fund's [`amit_from`](#listings) are skipped: it was an ordinary trust then, reported from its income rows. `amit_cash_alerts`, `e4_alerts`, and `amit_adjustment_alerts` are that cross-check's, the [E4 cross-check](#tax-deferred-e4-cross-check)'s, and the [AMIT adjustment cross-check](#amit-adjustment-cross-check)'s own alerts, each filtered to the year, and `rollover_alerts` is the [rollover consistency](#rollover-consistency) report's — deliberately **not** filtered, since a rollover from an earlier year is exactly the one whose stale carried cost base this year's disposals are costed on. `complete` is true only when all five are empty. The last two matter most here: an AMIT adjustment gap and a stale rollover figure both distort the disposal schedule's cost base, this report's central figure.
- **`disposals`** — every parcel disposed of in the year (ordinary Sells and rights sales/lapses), grouped by listing with per-listing and grand totals. Each parcel row carries: acquisition date, the trade's provenance-labelled acquisition method (Buy, DRP reinvestment, rights exercise, scrip exchange, etc. — the same labelling the [listing activity](#listing-activity) ledger uses) and contract note references; units, buy price, brokerage/GST; the initial cost base and, itemised underneath it, one row per cost-base adjustment (AMIT reduction, return-of-capital reduction, or an informational nil-amount split/consolidation rebase) with its own date, reference, and per-unit figure — new detail: [`domain::cost_base`](#fx-conversion) only exposes these as cumulative totals everywhere else; sale date, price, and net proceeds; the resulting gain/loss, days held, discount eligibility, and the CGT-discounted gain. A non-AUD parcel additionally carries its native currency and, for each side, **the rate the figure beside it was actually converted at** (see [FX conversion](#fx-conversion)): the parcel's own rate on the buy side and the disposal's own rate on the sell side — a deliberate `spot_fx_rate` override where one was entered, the ATO monthly rate otherwise, and the record's `fx_rate` fallback where that month has no imported rate. So the printed rate always reproduces the printed AUD figure, which is the point of printing it in an archived document. A listing's group heading names the ticker as at its **most recent disposal in the year** — so a document keeps reading the way the broker statement did across a [rename](#listings), unlike every other report, which shows the current ticker.
- **`cgt_summary`** — the year's assessable-gain position in the ATO worksheet layout (`docs/ato/personal-investors-guide-managed-fund-distributions.md`): short-term (Other method) gains less losses offset; long-term (Discount method) gains — split into realised/E10/G1 gains and the grossed-up AMMA discount-method distribution component, on separate lines — less losses offset, less the 50% CGT concession, to the final Capital Gain; plus the brought-forward/carried-forward loss position and the informational CGT event E10/G1 gain lines. `null` when the year has neither gain/loss activity recorded nor a capital loss brought forward into it (matching the [net capital gain](#net-capital-gain) report's own behaviour of only emitting a year row when there's something to report). A **quiet** year that carries a loss forward is not null: it prints all zeros but for the brought-forward/carried-forward pair, so the archived document states that year's label 18V figure (see [Which years get a record](#net-capital-gain)). The year picker (`GET /reports/tax-report/years`) offers such a year, since it is built from that same year walk.
- **`income`** — per-record detail (not just year totals) across: `trust_income` (trust income rows other than an AMIT's cash rows — per the [listing](#listings)'s status **in each row's own financial year**, so a converted fund's pre-conversion distributions print here, behind the tax-summary total that counts them) and `amma_statements` (the full AMMA component breakdown for the year); `dividends` (non-trust income rows carrying Item 11 content — i.e. any non-zero Australian-sourced component: franked or unfranked amount, franking credit, LIC deduction, conduit foreign income, or TFN withholding — each carrying its franking entitlement `franking_status` — `entitled`, `denied`, or `exempt_small_shareholder` — from the [franking at-risk](#franking-at-risk) walk, so a denied credit is visible on the row, not just missing from a year total; a foreign company's dividend, entered with only `foreign_source_income` set, carries no Item 11 content and so is excluded here — it appears in `foreign_income` instead — rather than printing as an all-zero row); `foreign_income` (every foreign-source amount — dividend/trust, AMMA, interest, the ESS foreign-source memo — with its foreign tax paid); `interest`; `ess`; and `deductions` (each carrying the `ticker` of the listing the expense was attributed to, `null` for a portfolio-wide one — after a rename, demerger, or worthless declaration the bare `listing_id` would be the only trace of which holding the fee was for, and the printed page would not even have that — plus its `destination` and that destination's `ato_label`, so the archived document says *where* each figure goes as well as what it was: `TrustDistributions`/`13Y`, `ForeignIncome`/`20M`, `ForeignDebt`/`D15`, or `DividendAndInterest`/`D7 / D8`, routed exactly as the [tax summary](#tax-summary)'s per-destination lines are). Every AUD figure here sums to exactly the matching [tax summary](#tax-summary) line for the year — with one deliberate exception, the dividend and trust rows' `conduit_foreign_income_aud`, which is a **memo** already contained in the same row's `unfranked_amount_aud` (see [Income](#income)) and so has no line of its own to sum to. Each row's `ticker` resolves **as at its own date** (pay date, AMMA year end, taxing point, or date incurred) across any [rename](#listings), for the same reason as the disposal schedule.
- **`tax_summary`** — the year's `TaxYearSummary` row (see [Tax summary](#tax-summary)) as `{ field, ato_label, value }` triples in CSV-export column order, reusing that endpoint's own ATO label mapping — one source of truth for the labels, shared with the CSV export.

Read consistency: the disposal schedule, CGT summary, and overall tax summary read on one transaction (a single consistent snapshot). The completeness section's two cross-checks and the per-record income/franking detail read on their own snapshots (advisory notes and drilldown rows alongside a total computed elsewhere — a rare interleaved write between them could only change whether an advisory note fires, never a reported dollar figure).

Print path: the web UI renders this as a plain semantic document (not the shared `filterableTable` — a print document has no business with a filter row, sort indicators, or a 50-row pager, which would silently print only the first page) with a Print / Save as PDF button (`window.print()`) over a dedicated `@media print` stylesheet. That stylesheet fixes the page to **A4 landscape** (so an archived PDF doesn't depend on a print-dialog setting) and lets document table cells wrap at a reduced print size, so a wide table compresses its columns onto the page instead of having its right-hand columns clipped — a money figure, date, quantity or price is never broken mid-token. **WebKit implements neither `@page` descriptor** (size or margin), so Safari prints at whatever orientation and margins its dialog is set to; the view says so beside the Print button, and a `@media print and (orientation: portrait)` block drops a point of type so the widest table (the 12-column disposal schedule) still fits — every column prints on a portrait page too, just denser. Choosing Landscape in Safari's dialog gives the roomiest result. The `amma_statements` breakdown is additionally rendered **transposed** — components down the page, one column per statement — because fifteen money components in one row cannot fit a page at a legible size; it reads the way the paper AMMA statement does.

### Exchange MIC validation

```
GET /reports/exchange_mic_validation
```

Validates each curated exchange's MIC against the `mic_registry` (the imported ISO 10383 list) — **non-blocking**: writes to `exchanges` are never rejected, this only surfaces MICs worth a second look. Returns one record per exchange (sorted by MIC) with fields: `mic`, `exchange_name`, `registry_status` (`ok` = active in the registry, `expired` = present but EXPIRED, `unknown` = no registry entry, i.e. a typo or the registry hasn't been imported yet), `iso_status` (raw ISO `ACTIVE`/`UPDATED`/`EXPIRED`, or null when unknown), and `expiry_date`. With an empty registry every exchange is `unknown`.

### FX coverage

```
GET /reports/fx_coverage
```

The FX counterpart of [settlement holiday coverage](#settlement-holiday-coverage) below, and **non-blocking** in the same way: writes are never rejected because of what is in here, and an empty report is the statement that every non-AUD amount converts at a published rate with no simplification in play. Returns one alert per affected record, each with `kind`, `record` (the table as the screens name it), `record_id`, `listing_id`/`ticker` (null for a portfolio-wide row), `date`, `currency`, `month` (the conversion month the alert is about) and a `detail` sentence naming what is at stake. Three kinds:
